Step 8.

Calculate total external work for an assumed deflection of Δ. Use one quarter of the roof slab due to symmetry in both directions. See Figure 4A6 (+) for yield lines and sectors.



Equivalent square column capital, CXc.

$$\begin{aligned}\boldsymbol{C}&=\left[\frac{\pi\boldsymbol{D}^2}{4}\right]^{1/2}=\left[\frac{\pi\left(45\right)^2}{4}\right]^{1/2}=39.9in say40\times40in\\\boldsymbol{W}_1&=\boldsymbol{W}_{tV}=r_u\left[\left(\frac{3L}{2}-\boldsymbol{X}\right)\left(\boldsymbol{X}\right)\frac{\boldsymbol{A}}{2}+\left(\frac{\boldsymbol{X}^2}{2}\right)\frac{\boldsymbol{A}}{3}\right]\\&=r_u\left[\left(\frac{3\times240}{2}-\boldsymbol{X}\right)\left(\boldsymbol{X}\right)\frac{\boldsymbol{A}}{2}+\left(\frac{\boldsymbol{X}^2}{2}\right)\frac{\boldsymbol{A}}{3}\right]\\\boldsymbol{W}_{it}&=\boldsymbol{W}_v=r_u\left[\boldsymbol{C}\left[\boldsymbol{H}-\boldsymbol{C}/2-\boldsymbol{X}\right]\frac{\boldsymbol{A}}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\left[\frac{3L}{2}-\boldsymbol{X}-\boldsymbol{C}\right]\boldsymbol{H}-\frac{\boldsymbol{C}}{2}-\boldsymbol{X}\left[\frac{2\boldsymbol{A}}{3}\right]\right]\\&=r_u\left[40\left(240-\frac{40}{2}-\boldsymbol{X}\right)\frac{\boldsymbol{A}}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{3\times240}{2}-\boldsymbol{X}-40\right)\left(240-\frac{40}{2}-\boldsymbol{X}\right)\left(\frac{2\boldsymbol{A}}{3}\right)\right]\\\boldsymbol{W}_{it}&=\boldsymbol{W}_{it}=r_u\left[\frac{\boldsymbol{C}}{2}\big(\boldsymbol{H}-\boldsymbol{C}\big)\frac{\boldsymbol{A}}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{3L}{2}-\boldsymbol{X}-\boldsymbol{C}\right)\left(\frac{\boldsymbol{H}-\boldsymbol{C}}{2}\right)\left(\frac{2\boldsymbol{A}}{3}\right)\right]\\&=r_u\left[\frac{40}{2}\big(240-40\big)\frac{\boldsymbol{A}}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\left[\frac{(3)\big(240)}{2}-\boldsymbol{X}-40\right]\left(\frac{240-40}{2}\right)\left(\frac{2\boldsymbol{A}}{3}\right)\right]\end{aligned}$$

From Equation 4-74.

$$\begin{aligned}\boldsymbol{W}&=\sum r_{u}\boldsymbol{A}\boldsymbol{\varDelta}\\\boldsymbol{W}&=\sum_{i=1}^{VI}\boldsymbol{W}_{i}=\frac{r_{u}\boldsymbol{\varDelta}}{3}(243200-320\boldsymbol{X})\end{aligned}$$

Calculate total internal work for the assumed deflection of Δ. Use one quarter of the roof slab due to symmetry in both directions. See Figure 4A$\dot{6}_{\textrm{(+)}}$ for angles of rotation.



$$\begin{aligned}\varTheta_{1H}&=\varTheta_{1V}=\frac{\varLambda}{X}\\\varTheta_{2H}&=\varTheta_{2V}=\frac{\varLambda}{H-C/2-X}=\frac{\varLambda}{240-40/2-X}=\frac{\varLambda}{220-X}\\\varTheta_{3H}&=\varTheta_{3V}=\frac{\varLambda}{(H-C)/2}=\frac{\varLambda}{(240-40)/2}=\frac{\varLambda}{100}\end{aligned}$$

Assume $0<X<3H/4=180$

$$\begin{aligned}E_{I}=E_{IV}=m_{1}^{-}\Theta_{1H}\left(\frac{3L-\overline{X}}{2}\right)+\frac{2}{3}m_{1}^{-}\Theta_{1H}\left(\frac{\overline{X}}{2}\right)+m_{2}^{+}\Theta_{1H}\left(\frac{\overline{L}}{2}\right)+\\m_{2}^{+}\Theta_{1H}\left(L-\frac{\overline{X}}{2}\right)+\frac{2}{3}m_{2}^{-}\Theta_{1H}\left(\frac{\overline{X}}{2}\right)\\=\frac{M_{OH}\varDelta}{(2)(240)\overline{X}}\left[0.494\left[3(240)-\frac{\overline{X}}{3}\right]+0.501(240)+0.334\left[2(240)-\frac{\overline{X}}{3}\right]\right]\\E_{II}=E_{V}=m_{6}^{+}\Theta_{2H}\left(\frac{\overline{L}}{2}\right)+m_{2}^{+}\Theta_{2H}\left(L-\overline{X}\right)+m_{7}^{-}\Theta_{2H}\left(\frac{\overline{L}}{2}\right)+m_{3}^{-}\Theta_{2H}\left(L-\overline{X}\right)\\=\frac{M_{OH}}{(2)(240)\overline{X}}\times\frac{\varDelta}{220-\overline{X}}\left[\left(0.501+1.011\right)240+2\left(0.334+0.337\right)(240-\overline{X})\right]\\E_{III}=E_{V1}=m_{9}^{+}\Theta_{3H}\left(\frac{\overline{L}}{2}\right)+m_{5}^{+}\Theta_{3H}\left(L-\overline{X}\right)+m_{7}^{-}\Theta_{3H}\left(\frac{\overline{L}}{2}\right)+m_{3}^{-}\Theta_{3H}\left(L-\overline{X}\right)\\=\frac{M_{OH}}{(2)(240)\overline{X}}\times\frac{\varDelta}{100}\left[\left(0.348+1.011\right)240+2\left(0.256+0.337\right)(240-\overline{X})\right]\end{aligned}$$

From Equation 4-75.

$$\begin{aligned}&E=\sum m\in I\\&E=\sum_{i=1}^{N}E_{i}=\frac{M_{OH}[139972.8+1331.76X-9.7832X^{2}+0.01186X^{3}]}{240X(220-X)}\\ \end{aligned}$$

$$\begin{aligned}\left(\frac{\bar{L}}{\bar{L}L_{1}}\right)_{i}&=\left(\frac{\bar{L}}{\bar{L}L_{1}}\right)_{V_{i}}=\frac{2}{3}X\left(\frac{\bar{L}L}{2}-X\right)+\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{\bar{X}^{2}}{2}\right)=\frac{2}{3}(116\left(\frac{3(240)}{2}-116\right)+\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{116^{2}}{2}\right)\\\left(\frac{\bar{L}}{\bar{L}L_{1}}\right)_{i}&=\left(\frac{\bar{L}}{\bar{L}L_{1}}\right)_{V_{i}}=\frac{2}{3}C\left(H-\frac{C}{2}-X\right)+\frac{3}{4}\left(H-\frac{C}{2}-X\right)\frac{3}{2}C-X\right)/2\\&=\frac{2}{3}(40\left(240-\frac{40}{2}-116\right)+\frac{3}{4}\left(240-\frac{40}{2}-116\right)\left(\frac{3(240)}{2}-40-116\right)/2\\\left(\frac{\bar{L}}{\bar{L}L_{1}}\right)_{ii}&=\left(\frac{\bar{L}}{\bar{L}L_{1}}\right)_{V_{i}}=\frac{2}{3}C\frac{\left(H-C\right)}{2}+\frac{3}{4}(H-C)\left(\frac{3\bar{L}}{2}-C-X\right)/4\\&=\frac{2}{3}(40)\frac{240-40}{2}+\frac{3}{4}(240-40)\left(\frac{3(240)}{2}-40-116\right)/4\\\sum_{i=1}^{N_{i}}\left(\frac{\bar{I}}{\bar{L}L_{1}}\right)_{i}&=86558.67in^{2}\\A_{i}&=A_{iv}=\frac{X}{2}\left(3L-X\right)=\frac{116}{2}(3\times240-116)\\A_{ii}&=A_{v}=\left(H-\frac{C}{2}-X\right)\left(\frac{3\bar{L}}{2}+C-X\right)/2\\&=\left(240-\frac{40}{2}-116\right)\left(\frac{3(240)}{2}+40-116\right)/2\\A_{iii}&=A_{vi}=\left(H-C\right)\left(\frac{3\bar{L}}{2}+C-X\right)/4\\&=\left(240-40\right)\left(\frac{3(240)}{2}+40-116\right)/4\\\sum_{i=1}^{N_{i}}A_{i}&=128,000.0in^{2}\\K_{LM}&=\frac{\sum\left(\frac{I}{\bar{L}L_{i}}\right)_{i}}{\sum A_{i}}=\frac{86558.67}{128,000.0}=0.676\quad(eq.3-59)\end{aligned}$$

$$p_{m14}^{+}=\frac{0.20}{12\times11.25}=0.0015=0.0015\ O.K.$$

$$\begin{aligned}&v_{c}=1.9(f_{c}^{\prime})^{1/2}+2500p\quad(\mathrm{Equ})\\&v_{cv}=1.9(4000)^{1/2}+2500(0.0031)=127.9\mathrm{psi}>125.9\mathrm{psi}\\ \end{aligned} ation 4-23)$$

Therefore, No stirrups or wall drop panel required.

# PROBLEM 4A-3. LACED ELEMENTS DESIGNED FOR IMPULSE-LARGE  DEFLECTIONS 

### Problem:Design an element subjected to an impulse load for a large deflection. 

## Procedure:

Step 1.Establish design parameters:

a.Impulse load and duration (Chapter 2).

b.Deflection criteria.

C.Geometry of element.

d.Support conditions.

e.Type of section available to resist blast, Type ll or Il depending upon the occurrence of spalling.



f.Materials to be used and corresponding static design strengths.

g.Dynamic increase factors (Table 4-1 (+)).

Step 2.Determine dynamic yield strength and dynamic ultimate strength of reinforcement.



Step 3.Determine dynamic design stress for the reinforcement according to the deflection range (support rotation) required by the desired protection level (Table 4-2 (+)).



Step 4.Determine optimum distribution of the reinforcement according to the deflection range considered (Section 4-33.4 and Figures 4-37 (+) and 4-38(+)). Step not necessary for one-way elements.



Step 5.Establish design equation for deflection range considered and type of section (Type Il or IIl) available.



Step 6.Determine impulse coefficient $C_{1}$ and/or $C_{u}$ for optimum $p_{V}/p_{H}$ ratio and L/H ratio.



Note: If the desired deflection $X_{m}$ is not equal to $X_{1}$ or $X_{u},$ determine yield line location (Figures 3-4 (+) through 3-20 (+)) for optimum $p_{V}/p_{H}$ ratio and L/H ratio and calculate $X_{m},X_{1}$ , and, if necessary,$X_{u}$ (Table 3-5 (+) or 3-6(+)).



Step 7.Substitute known parameters into equation of Step 5 to obtain relationship between $p_{H}$ and $d_{c}$ .



<!-- batch 25 | page 2 -->

Step 8.Assume value of $d_{c}$ and calculate $p_{H}$ and from optimum $p_{V}/p_{H}$ calculate $p_{V_{1}}$ Select bar sizes and spacings necessary to furnish required reinforcement (see Section for limitations).



Step 9.For actual distribution of flexural reinforcement, establish yield line location (Figures 3-4 (+) through 3-20 (+)).



Note:

$$\frac{L}{H}\left[\frac{M_{V N}+M_{V P}}{M_{H N}+M_{H P}}\right]^{1/2}=\frac{L}{H}\left[\frac{p_{V}}{P_{H}}\right]^{1/2}=\frac{L}{H}\left[\frac{A_{s V}}{A_{s H}}\right]^{1/2}$$

since :

$$M_{N}=M_{P}=p d_{c}^{2}f_{d s}=\frac{A_{s}}{b}d_{c}f_{d s},$$

Step 10.Determine the ultimate shear stress at distance $d_{c}$ from the support in both the vertical $(v_{u v}$ ·from Equation 4-119) and horizontal $(V_{u H}$ from Equation 4118) directions where the coefficients $C_{V}$ and $C_{H}$ are determined from Figures 4-39 (+) through 4-52 (+) (see Section 4-35.2 for an explanation of the figures and parameters involved).



Step 11.Determine the shear capacity $v_{c}$ of the concrete in both the vertical and horizontal directions (use Equation 4-21).



Step 12.Select lacing method to be used (Figure 4-91 (+)). (Note: Lacing making an angle of 45with longitudinal reinforcement is most efficient.)

Step 13.Determine the required lacing bar sizes for both the vertical and horizontal directions from Equation 4-26 where the parameters $b_{l}$ and $s_{I}$ are determined from the lacing method used (Figure 4-91 (+)), and the angle of inclination of the lacing bars α is obtained from Figure 4-15 (+). The lacing bar size $d_{b}$ must be assumed in order to compute $d_{I}$ and $R_{I}$ 

(Note: See Section 4-18.3 for limitations imposed upon the design of the lacing).



Step 14.Determine required thickness $T_{c}$ for assumed,$d_{c},$ selected flexural and lacing bar sizes, and required concrete cover. Adjust $T_{c}$ to the nearest whole inch and calculate the actual $d_{c}$ 



Step 15.Check flexural capacity based on either impulse or deflection. Generally,lacing bar sizes do not have to be checked since they are not usually affected by a small change in $d_{c}$ 



a.Check of impulse. Compute actual impulse capacity of the element using the equation determined in Step 5 and compare with anticipated blast load, Repeat design (from Step 8 on) if capacity is less than required.



<!-- batch 25 | page 3 -->

b.Check of deflection. Compute actual maximum deflection of the element using equation determined in Step 5 and compare with deflection permitted by design criteria. Repeat design (from Step 8on) if actual deflection is greater than that permitted.

Step 16.Determine whether correct procedure has been used by first computing the response time of the element $t_{m}$ (time to reach maximum deflection)from Equation 3-95 or 3-96, depending on the deflection range considered in the design, and then compare response time $t_{m}$ with duration of load $\mathfrak{t}_{\circ}$ ,For elements to be designed for impulse,$t_{m}>3$ to.

Step 17.Determine the ultimate support shear in both the vertical $(V_{s V}$ from Equation 4-122) and horizontal $(V_{s H}$ from Equation 4-121) directions. The coefficients $C_{s V}$ and $C_{s H}$ are determined from Table 4-15 (+) and Figures 4-53 (+) through 4-56$(\pm)$ (see Section 4-35.3 for an explanation of the figures and parameters involved).



Step 18.Determine the required diagonal bar sizes for the vertical and horizontal (intersecting elements may control) directions from Equation 4-30.Diagonal bars should have the same spacing as the flexural reinforcement (Figure 4-95 (+)).



Note: To obtain the most economical design repeat the above steps for several wall thicknesses and compare costs. Percentages of reinforcement may be used to reduce the amount of calculations. In determining the required quantities of reinforcement, lapping of the bars should be considered.

$$\begin{aligned}&\mathrm{From}\;\mathrm{Table}\;3-6\;(+)\;\mathrm{for}\;x>H;\\&X_{1}=H\tan12^{\circ}=120(0.2125)=25.5\;\mathrm{in}\\&X_{u}=x\tan\theta_{max}+\left(\frac{L}{2}-x\right)\tan\left[\theta_{max}-\tan^{-1}\frac{\tan\theta_{max}}{x/H}\right]\\&=(129)\tan12^{\circ}+(168-129)\tan\left[12^{\circ}-\tan^{-1}\frac{\tan12^{\circ}}{129/120}\right]\\&=27.42+0.56=27.98\;\mathrm{in}\\ \end{aligned}$$

### PROBLEM 4A-4. ELEMENTS DESIGNED FOR IMPULSE — LIMITED DEFLECTIONS 

### Problem:Design an element which responds to the impulse loading of a closein detonation. 

## Procedure:

Step 1.Establish design parameters:

a.Blast loads including pressure-time relationship (Chapter 2).

b.Deflection criteria.

C.Structural configuration including geometry and support conditions.

d.Type of cross section available depending upon the occurrence of spalling and/or crushing of the concrete cover.

Step 2.Select cross section of element including thickness and concrete cover over the reinforcement. Also determine the static design stresses of concrete and reinforcing steel (Section 4-12).



Step 3.Determine dynamic increase factors for both concrete and reinforcement from Table 4-1 (+). Using the above DIFs and the static design stresses of Step 2, calculate the dynamic strength of materials.

Step 4.Determine the dynamic design stresses using Table 4-2 (+) and the results from Step 3.



Step 5.Assume vertical and horizontal reinforcement bars to yield the optimum steel ratio. The steel ratio is optimum when the resulting yield lines make an angle of 45 degrees with the supports.



Step 6.Calculate $d_{e}$ (d or $d_{c}$ depending upon the type of cross section available to resist the blast load) for both the positive and negative moments in both the vertical and horizontal directions. Determine the reinforcing ratios. Also check for the minimum steel ratios from Table 4-3 (+)

Step 7.Using the area of reinforcement and the value of $d_{e}$ from Step 6, and the dynamic design stress of Step 4, calculate the moment capacity (Section 4-17) of both the positive and negative reinforcement. Also calculate the $p_{V}/p_{H}$ ratio and compare to the optimum steel ratio from Step 5.

Step 8.Establish values of $K_{E}$ ,$X_{E}$ and $r_{u}$ similar to the procedures of Problem 4A1, Steps 8 to 18.



Step 9.Determine the load-mass factor $K_{L M_{1}}$ ,for elastic, elasto-plastic and plastic ranges from Table 3-13 (+) and Figure 3-44 (+). The average load mass factor is obtained by taking the average $K_{L M}$ ,for the elastic and elastoplastic ranges and averaging this value with the $K_{L M}$ of the plastic range.

<!-- batch 25 | page 17 -->

In addition, calculate the unit mass of the element (account for reduced concrete thickness if spalling is anticipated) and multiply this unit mass by $K_{L M}$ for the element to obtain the effective unit mass of the element.

Step 10.Using the effective mass of Step 9 and the equivalent stiffness of Step 8,calculate the natural period of vibration $T_{N}$ from Equation 3-60.

Step 11.Determine the response chart parameters:

a.Peak pressure P (Step 1).

b.Peak resistance $r_{u}$ (Step 8).

C.Duration of load T (Step 1.

d.Natural period of vibration $T_{N}$ (Step 10).

Also calculate the ratios of peak pressure P to peak resistance $r_{u}$ and duration Tto period of vibration $T_{N}$ . Using these ratios and the response charts of Chapter 3, determine the value of $X_{m}/X_{E}$ Compute the value of $X_{m}$ 



Step 12.Determine the support rotation corresponding to the value of $X_{m}$ from Step 11 using the equations of Table 3-6 (+). Compare this value to maximum permissible support rotation of Step 1, and if found to be satisfactory,proceed to Step 13. If comparison is unsatisfactory, repeat Steps 2 to 12.

Step 13.Using the ultimate resistance of Step 8, the values of $d_{e}$ of Step 6 and the equations of Table 4-6 (+/+) or 4-7 (+/+)(Table 3-10 (+) or 3-11 (+) if shear at support is required), calculate the ultimate diagonal tension shear stress at a distance $d_{e}$ from each support (or at each support). Also,calculate the shear capacity of the concrete from Equation 4-23. If the capacity of the concrete is greater than that produced by the load,minimum shear reinforcement must be used. However, if the shear produced by the load is greater than the capacity of the concrete, then shear reinforcement in excess of the minimum required must be provided.Also check for maximum spacing of shear reinforcement.

Step 14.Using the equations of Table 3-9 (+), 3-10 (+) or 3-11 (+) and the ultimate resistance of Step 8, calculate the shear at the supports. Determine the required area of diagonal bars using Equation 4-30. However, if section Type Iis used, then the minimum diagonal bars must be provided.

<!-- batch 25 | page 18 -->

### EXAMPLE 4A-4. ELEMENTS DESIGNED FOR IMPULSE - LIMITED DEFLECTIONS 

Required:Design the side wall of cubicle with no roof or front wall and subject to the effects of a detonation of an explosive within the cubicle.

## Solution:

Step 1:Given:

a.Pressure-time loading (Figure 4A-12 (+))

b.Maximum support rotation equal to 2 degrees.

C.$L=180$ in,$H=144$ in and fixed on two sides (Figure 4A-12).

d.Type Ill cross section.

<div style="text-align: center;">Figure 4A-12</div>


<div style="text-align: center;"><img src="../pages/batch 25/imgs/img_in_image_box_197_703_995_970.jpg" alt="Image" width="67%" /></div>


Step 2.Select element thickness and static stress of reinforcement and concrete (Error! Reference source not found. (+)).



<div style="text-align: center;"><img src="../pages/batch 25/imgs/img_in_image_box_165_1065_657_1418.jpg" alt="Image" width="41%" /></div>




$f_{c}^{\prime}=4,000\mathrm{p s i}$ 

Ássume $T_{c}=22$ in and concrete cover is as shown in Error!Reference source not found..

<!-- batch 25 | page 19 -->

Step 3.Determine dynamic stresses.

a.Dynamic increase factors, DIF (from Table 4-1 (+)).

Concrete:

Diagonal Tension  - 1.00

Reinforcement:

Bending -1.23

Diagonal Tension -1.10

Direct Shear -1.10

b.Dynamic strength of materials.

Concrete $(\pmb{f}_{d c}^{r})_{1}$ 

Diagonal Tension $1.00(4,000)=4,000\ \mathrm{ps}i$ 

Reinforcement $(\pmb{f}_{d y})$ 

Bending 

$$1.23(66,000)=81,180\mathrm{ps}$$

Diagonal Tension $1.10(66,000)=72,600\mathrm{ps}$ 

Direct Shear $1.10(66,000)=72,600\mathrm{ps}$ 

Step 4.Dynamic design stress from Table 4-2 (+)

Concrete $(\pmb{f}_{d c}^{r})$ :

Diagonal Tension - 4,000 psi 

Reinforcement $(f_{d s}=f_{d v}\mathrm{f o r}0<2)$ 

Bending - 81,180 psi 

Diagonal Tension - 72,600 psi 

Direct Shear - 72,600 psi 

Step 5.Determine the optimum steel ratio $p_{V}/p_{H}.\mathrm{S e t}x=H$ to obtain 45 degree yield lines.



$$\frac{x}{L}=\frac{H}{L}=\frac{144}{180}=0.80$$

From Figure 3-4 (+),

$$\frac{L}{H}\left(\frac{M_{V P}}{M_{H N}+M_{H P}}\right)=1.08$$

<!-- batch 25 | page 20 -->

Therefore,

$$\frac{M_{VP}}{M_{HIN}+M_{HP}}=\left[\frac{1.08\times144}{180}\right]^2=0.75$$

or 

$$M_{V P}/M_{H N}=1.50$$

Try No. 7 bars at 8 in o.c. in the vertical direction and No.6 bars at 8 in o.c. in the horizontal direction.



Calculate $d_{c}$ and the steel ratios for each direction.

Assume No. 3 stirrups.

$$\mathrm{d}_{\mathrm{CV}}=22-(2\times0.375)-0.75-1.5-(2\times0.875/2)=18.125\mathrm{~in}$$

$$\mathrm{d}_{\mathrm{CH}}=18.125-(2\times0.875/2)-(2\times0.75/2)=16.50\mathrm{~in}$$

$$p_{v}=\frac{A_{sV}}{bd_{cV}}=\frac{0.60}{8\times18.125}=0.0041>p_{min}=\frac{1.875\sqrt{f_{c}^{\prime}}}{f_{v}}=\frac{1.875\sqrt{4.000}}{66.000}$$

$$0.0018,\mathrm{soO.K.}$$

$$p_{H}=\frac{A_{sH}}{bd_{cH}}=\frac{0.44}{8\times16.50}=0.0033>0.0018,\mathrm{so}\mathrm{O}.\mathrm{K}.$$

Step 7.

Calculate the moment capacity of both the positive and negative reinforcement in both directions (Equation 4-19).

$$\begin{aligned}&M_{u}=\frac{A_{s}f_{ds}d_{c}}{b}\\ &M_{vN}=M_{VP}=\frac{0.60(81,180)(18.125)}{8}=110,354\mathrm{in}-\mathrm{lbs}/\mathrm{in}\\ &M_{HN}=M_{HP}=\frac{0.44(81,180)(16.50)}{8}=73,671\mathrm{in}-\mathrm{lbs}/\mathrm{in}\\ &M_{vN}/M_{HN}=110,354/73,671\approx1.5=1.5\mathrm{from}\mathrm{step}5\mathrm{O}.\mathrm{K}.\\ \end{aligned}$$

Step 8.Using the procedure in example 4A-1, Steps 8 through 18 and the moment capacities from Step 7. establish the values of $K_{E},X_{E}\mathrm{a n d}r_{u}$ 

$$K_{E}=36.7\ \mathrm{psi/in}$$

<!-- batch 25 | page 21 -->

Calculate the effective mass of the element.

a.Load mass factors (Table 3-13 (+) and Figure 3-44 (+))

$$x/L=0.80$$

elastic range $K_{L M}=0.65$ 

elasto-plastic range:

one simple edge $K_{L M}=0.66$ 

two simple edges $K_{L M}=0.66$ 

plastic range $K_{L M}=0.54$ 

$K_{L M}$ (average elastic and elasto-plastic values)

$$\frac{0.65+0.66+0.66}{3}=0.66$$

$K_{L M}$ (average elastic and plastic values)

$$\frac{0.66+0.54}{2}=0.60$$

b.Unit mass of element:

Using the larger $d_{c}$ as the thickness of the element 

Due to spalling (Type Ill cross section) available thickness equals 

$$T_{c}=d_{c}=18.125\ln $$

$$m=\frac{w_{c}d_{c}}{g}=\frac{150(18.125)10^{6}}{32.2\times12\times1728}=4.072\mathrm{ps}i-\mathrm{ms}^{2}/\mathrm{in}$$

C.Effective unit mass of element:

$$m_{e}=K_{LM}m=0.60\left(4,072\right)=2,443\ \mathrm{psi-ms^{2}/in}$$

Step 10.Calculate the natural period of vibration.

$$T_{N}=2\;\pi\;(m_{e}/K_{E})^{1/2}\;\mathrm{~\mu~{~}~}\;\mathrm{~\mu~{~}~}\;T_{N}=2\;(3.14)\;(2443/36.7)^{1/2}=51.2\;\mathrm{m s}.$$

(Equation 3-60)

a.Response chart parameters:

Peak pressure,$P=1485\mathrm{p s i}(\mathrm{S t e p}1)$ 

Peak resistance,$r_{u}=35.53$ : psi (Step 8)

Period of vibration $T_{N}=51.2$ ms (Step 10)

$$P/r_{u}=1485/35.53=41.8$$

$$T/T_{N}=1.2/51.2=0.023$$

$$\begin{aligned}\mathsf{b.}\quad&\mathsf{From Figure3-64a(+):}\\&X_m/X_E=5.0\\&X_m=5.0\times0.968=4.84\mathsf{in}\end{aligned}$$

Step 12.Check support rotation (Table 3-6 (+)).

Since $x=H=144$ in and $0<X_{m}<X_{1}$ 

$$\begin{aligned}&X_{m}=x\tan\Theta_{H}\\&\tan\Theta_{H}=4.84/144\\ \end{aligned}$$

$$\Theta_{H}=1.93^{\circ}<2^{\circ}\mathrm{a s s u m e d s e c t i o n i s O.K.}$$

Step 13.Check diagonal tension at supports (internal loading).

a.Calculate ultimate shear stresses at support by dividing the values of the support shear from Table 3-10 (+) by their respective $d_{c}$ ,

$$V_{sH}=3r_{u}x/5=3\times35.53\times144/5=3,070\ lb/in $$

$$\frac{3r_{u}H\left(2-\frac{x}{L}\right)}{6-\frac{x}{L}}=\frac{3(35.53)(144)(2-0.80)}{6-0.80}=3.5421b/in $$

b.Allowable shear stresses 

(Equation 4-23)

$$v_{c}=1.9\;(f_{c}^{\prime})^{1/2}+2500\;p\leq3.5\;(f_{c}^{\prime})^{1/2}=221.4\;\mathrm{p s i}$$

where p is the steel ratio at the support.

$$\begin{aligned}&v_{CH}=1.9\left(4000\right)^{1/2}+2500\left(0.0033\right)=128.4\mathrm{psi}<221.4\mathrm{psi}\\&v_{CV}=1.9\left(4000\right)^{1/2}+2500\left(0.0041\right)=130.4\mathrm{psi}<221.4\mathrm{psi}\\ \end{aligned}$$

<!-- batch 25 | page 23 -->

C.Required area of single leg stirrups.

$$A_{V}=\frac{(V_{u}-V_{c})b s}{0.85f_{d s}^{\prime}}$$

$$v_{uH}-v_{cH}=186.1-128.4=57.7<0.85\ v_{cH}=108.8\ psi $$

Use 0.85$V_{C H}$ as minimum.

$$v_{uV}-v_{cV}=195.4-130.4=65.0<0.85v_{cV}=110.8\ ps i $$

Use 0.85$v_{c v}$ as minimum.

Tie every reinforcing bar intersection, therefore,

$b=s=8\;\mathrm{i n}<d_{0}/2$ (maximum spacing) O.K.

$$\begin{aligned}&A_{vH}=\frac{108.8\times8\times8}{0.85\times72.600}=0.11\ln^2\\ &\quad minimum A_v=0.0015bs=0.10\ln^2<0.11O.K.\\ &A_{vv}=\frac{110.8\times8\times8}{0.85\times72.600}=0.11\ln^2\\ &\quad minimum A_v=0.0015bs=0.10\ln^2<0.11O.K.\\ \end{aligned}$$

The area of No. 3 bar is 0.11$\mathsf{i n}^{2}$ , so bar assumed in Step 6 is O.K.

# PROBLEM 4A-5. ELEMENTS DESIGNED FOR IMPULSE- COMPOSITE  CONSTRUCTION 

### Problem:Design a composite (concrete-sand-concrete) wall to resist a given blast output for incipient failure. 

## Procedure:

Step 1.Establish design parameters.

a.Structure configuration.

b.Charge weight.

C.Blast impulse load (Chapter 2).

d.Thicknesses of concrete and sand portions of wall.

e.Blast impulse resisted by concrete panels.

f.Density of concrete and sand.

Step 2.Determine scaled thicknesses of concrete and sand using:

$$\overline{{T}}_{c}=T_{c}/\boldsymbol{W}^{1/3}\mathrm{a n d}\overline{{T}}_{s}=T_{s}/\boldsymbol{W}^{1/3}$$

Step 3.Determine scaled blast impulse resisted by each concrete panel using:

$$\begin{aligned}&\overline{{i}}_{bd}={i}_{bd}/\boldsymbol{W}^{1/3}\quad(donor panel)\\ &\overline{{i}}_{ba}={i}_{ba}/\boldsymbol{W}^{1/3}\quad(acceptor panel)\\ \end{aligned}$$

Step 4.Correct scaled blast impulse resisted by concrete (Step 3) to account for the increased mass produced by the sand and the reduction of the concrete mass produced by spalling and scabbing of the concrete panels using:

$$\begin{array}{r l}&{\left(\mathrm{C o r r.}\right)\quad\overline{{i}}_{b d}=\overline{{i}}_{b d}\left[\frac{\displaystyle\frac{T_{c}+d_{c}}{2}+\left(\displaystyle\frac{w_{s}}{w_{c}}\right)\left(\displaystyle\frac{T_{s}}{2}\right)}{d_{c}}\right]^{1/2}}\\ &{\left(\mathrm{C o r r.}\right)\quad\overline{{i}}_{b a}=\overline{{i}}_{b a}\left[\frac{\displaystyle\frac{T_{c}+d_{c}}{2}+\left(\displaystyle\frac{w_{s}}{w_{c}}\right)\left(\displaystyle\frac{T_{s}}{2}\right)}{d_{c}}\right]^{1/2}}\end{array}$$

<!-- batch 25 | page 25 -->

Step 5.Determine scaled blast impulse atenuated by acceptor panel and the sand $\bar{I}_{a}$ Figure 4-57 (+) or 4-58 (+), for $w_{s}$ equal to 85 and 100 pcf.respectively.



Step 6.Calculate total impulse resisted by the wall using:

$$\bar{\dot{I}}_{b t}=\bar{\dot{I}}_{a}+\bar{\dot{I}}_{b d}$$

Step 7.Compare blast impulse which is resisted by wall to that of the applied blast loads.



<!-- batch 25 | page 26 -->

# EXAMPLE 4A-5. ELEMENTS DESIGNED FOR IMPULSE - COMPOSITE  CONSTRUCTION 

Required:Design the composite wall shown below for incipient failure conditions.



<div style="text-align: center;">Figure 4A-14</div>


<div style="text-align: center;"><img src="../pages/batch 25/imgs/img_in_image_box_313_505_694_733.jpg" alt="Image" width="31%" /></div>


<div style="text-align: center;">ELEVATION </div>


<div style="text-align: center;"><img src="../pages/batch 25/imgs/img_in_image_box_743_507_856_732.jpg" alt="Image" width="9%" /></div>


<div style="text-align: center;">SECTION </div>


Step 1.Given:

a.Structural configuration as shown in Figure 4A-14 (+)

b.$W=1.000lbs$ 

C.$i_{b}=4,800\mathrm{p s i-m s}(\mathrm{C h a p t e r}2)$ 

d.

$$T_{c}=1\ ft,\ T_{s}=2\ ft,\ and\ d_{c}=0.833\ ft $$

e.$i_{b d}=i_{b a}=1,500\;\mathrm{p s i-m s}\;(\mathrm{S e c t i o n}\;4\mathrm{-}33)$ 

f.

$$w_{c}=150\;\mathrm{p c f}\;\mathrm{a n d}\;w_{s}=100\;\mathrm{p c f}$$

Step 2.Scaled thicknesses of concrete and sand:

$$\begin{aligned}\overline{T_{c}}&=T_{c}/\boldsymbol{W}^{1/3}=1/(1000)^{1/3}=0.1\mathrm{ft}/\mathrm{lb}^{1/3}\\\overline{T_{s}}&=T_{s}/\boldsymbol{W}^{1/3}=2/(1000)^{1/3}=0.2\mathrm{ft}/\mathrm{lb}^{1/3}\end{aligned}$$

Step 3.Scaled blast impulse resisted by individual concrete panels.

$$\bar{i}_{b d}=\bar{i}_{b a}=\frac{1500}{(1000)^{1/3}}=150\mathrm{p s i-m s/I b^{1/3}}$$

<!-- batch 25 | page 27 -->

Step 4.Correction of scaled impulse resisted by concrete panel used in composite walls.



$$\begin{aligned}\overline{\left(Corr.\right)}\overline{I}_{bd}&=\overline{I}_{bd}\left[\frac{\frac{T_c+d_c}{2}+\left(\frac{w_s}{w_c}\right)\left(\frac{T_s}{2}\right)}{d_c}\right]^{1/2}\\&=150\left[\frac{\frac{1.0+0.833}{2}+\frac{100}{150}\left(\frac{2}{2}\right)}{0.833}\right]^{1/2}\\&=207\mathrm{psi}-\mathrm{ms/lb}^{1/3}=\overline{I}_{ba}\left(\mathrm{corr.}\right)\end{aligned}$$

Step 5.Scaled blast impulse attenuated by acceptor panel and sand.

$$\bar{i}_{a}=280\mathrm{p s i-m s/l b}$$

Step 6.Total scaled blast impulse resisted by wall.

$$\bar{I}_{bt}=\bar{I}_a+\bar{I}_{bd}=280+207=487\mathrm{psi-ms/lb^{1/3}}$$

Step 7.Comparison of wall capacity and applied blast load.

$$\bar{i}_{\mathrm{b t}}=487\approx\bar{i}_{\mathrm{b}}=480~\mathrm{p s i-m s/l b^{1/3}}\quad\mathrm{O.K.}$$

<!-- batch 25 | page 28 -->

### PROBLEM 4A-6. DESIGN OF A BEAM IN FLEXURE 

### Problem:Design an interior beam of a roof subjected to an overhead blast load. 

## Solution:

Step 1.Establish design parameters:

a.Structural configuration.

b.Pressure-time loading.

C.Maximum allowable support rotation.

d.Material properties 

Step 2.From Table 4-1 (+), determine the dynamic increase factors, DIF. For the deflection criteria given in Step 1c, find the equation for the dynamic design stress from Table 4-2 (+). Using the DIF and the material properties from Step 1d, calculate the dynamic design stresses.

Step 3.Assuming reinforcing steel and concrete cover, calculate the distance from the extreme compression fiber to the centroid of the tension reinforcement, d.



Step 4.Calculate the reinforcement ratio of the steel assumed in Step 3. Check that this ratio is greater than the minimum reinforcement required by Equations 4-138 and 4-138a but less than the maximum reinforcement permitted by Equation 4-132.



Step 5.Using Equations 4-129 and 4-130, the dynamic design stresses from Step 2, and the value of d from Step 3. calculate the ultimate moment capacity of the beam.



Step 6.Compute the ultimate unit resistance of the beam using the moment capacity of Step 4 and an equation from Table 3-1 (+).

Step 7.Calculate the modulus of elasticity of concrete Ec and steel Es (Equations 4-4 and 4-5, respectively) and the modular ratio n (Equation 4-6).Determine the average moment of inertia $I_{a}$ of the beam according to Section 4-15.



Step 8.From Table 3-8 (+), find the correct equation for the equivalent elastic stiffness KE.Evaluate this equation using the values of E and $I_{a}$ from Step 7.



Step 9.With the ultimate resistance from Step 6 and the stiffness $K_{E}$ from Step 8,use Equation 3-36 to calculate the equivalent elastic deflection $X_{E}$ 

<!-- batch 25 | page 29 -->

Step 10.Find the values for the load-mass factor $K_{L M}$ in the elastic, elasto-plastic and plastic ranges from Table 3-12 (+). Average these values according to Section 3-17.4 to determine the value of $K_{L M}$ to be used in design.

Step 11.Determine the natural period of vibration $T_{N}$ using Equation 3-60,$K_{L M}$ from Step 10,$K_{E}$ from Step 8 and the mass of the beam. The mass includes 20percent of the adjacent slabs.



Step 12.Calculate the non-dimensional parameters $T/T_{N}$ and $r_{u}/P$ . Using the appropriate response chart by Chapter 3 determine the ductility ratio,$\mu.$ 

Step 13.Compute the maximum deflection $X_{m}$ using the ductility ratio from Step 12and $X_{E}$ from Step 9. Calculate the support rotation corresponding to $X_{m}$ using an equation from Table 3-5 (+). Compare this rotation with the maximum allowable rotation of Step 1c.



Step 14.Verify that the ultimate support shear $V_{s}$ given in Table 3-9 (+) does not exceed the maximum shear permitted by Equation 4-142. If it does, the size of the beam must be increased and Steps 2 through 13 repeated.

Step 15.Calculate the diagonal tension stress $v_{u}$ from Equation 4-139 and check that it does not exceed $10(\bar{\boldsymbol{\mathit{f}}}_{d c}^{\prime})^{1/2}$ 



Step 16.Using the dynamic concrete strength $\pmb{f}_{d c}$ from Step 2 and Equation 4-140,calculate the shear capacity of the unreinforced web,$v_{c}$ 

Step 17.Design the shear reinforcement using Equation 4-140, and the excess shear stress $(V_{u}-V_{c})$ or the shear capacity of concrete $v_{c}^{\prime}$ whichever is greater.



Step 18.Check that the shear reinforcement meets the minimum area and maximum spacing requirements of Section 4-39.4.

Step 19.With $T/T_{N}$ and $X_{m}/X_{E}$ from Step 12, enter Figure 3-268 (+) and read the required resistance of the beam in rebound.



Step 20.Repeat Steps 3 through 6 to satisfy the required rebound resistance.

<!-- batch 25 | page 30 -->

### EXAMPLE 4A-6. DESIGN OF A BEAM IN FLEXURE 

### Required:Design of an interior of a roof beam subjected to an overhead blast load. 

## Solution:

Step 1.Given:

a.Structural configuration is shown in Figure 4A-15a (+)

b.Pressure-time loading is shown Figure 4A-15c (+).

C.Maximum support rotation of two degrees.

d.Yield stress of reinforcing steel,$f_{y}=66,000\;\mathrm{p s i}$ 

Concrete compressive strength,$f_{c}^{\prime}=4,000\mathrm{p s}$ i 

Weight of concrete,$w=150~\mathrm{{l b s/f t^{3}}}$ 

Step 2.

a.Dynamic increase factors from Table $4{\scriptstyle-1}\underline{{(\pm)}}$ for intermediate and low pressure range.



Reinforcing steel- bending,$DIF\qquad=1.17$ 

- direct shear,$DIF_{ 説認 }=1.10$ 

Concrete -compression,$DIF_{ 證證 }=1.19$ 

- direct shear,$DIF\qquad=1.10$ 

- diagonal tension,$DIF=1.00$ 

b.From Table 4-2 (+), for $\mathcal{O}_{m}\leq2^{\circ}$ ：

$$\boldsymbol{f}_{d s}=\boldsymbol{f}_{d v}$$

C.Dynamic design stresses from Equation 4-3.

Reinforcing steel 

$$f_{d y}=1.17\times66,000$$

$$\begin{aligned}&f_{dy}&=1.00\times66,000\\&&=66,000\mathrm{psi}\\ \end{aligned}$$

<!-- batch 25 | page 31 -->

Concrete 

$$\begin{aligned}f-cmpression\quad f_{dc}^{\prime}&=1.19\times4,000\\&=4,760\;ps\\ f-direct shear\quad f_{dc}^{\prime}&=1.10\times4,000\\&=4,400\;ps\\ f-diagonal tension\quad f_{dy}&=1.00\times4,000\\&=4,000\;ps\\\end{aligned}$$

Step 3.Assume 5 No. 6 bars for bending:

$$A_{s}=5\times0.44=2.20\ln^{2}$$

For concrete cover and beam sections see Figure 4A-15b (+)

<!-- batch 25 | page 32 -->

<div style="text-align: center;">Figure 4A-15</div>


<div style="text-align: center;"><img src="../pages/batch 25/imgs/img_in_image_box_216_318_991_1495.jpg" alt="Image" width="65%" /></div>


<div style="text-align: center;">4A-66</div>


<!-- batch 25 | page 33 -->

Step 4.

Check reinforcement requirements:

a.Calculate d negative (support) and positive (mid-span) for checking bending reinforcement ratios.



$$\begin{aligned}&d=h-d^{\prime}(cover)-\phi^{\prime}(lie)-\frac{\phi}{2}(Bending Bar)\\ &d_{N}=30-2-0.5-0.75/2=27.125in\\ &d_{p}=30-1.5-0.5-0.75/2=27.625in\\ \end{aligned}$$

b.Calculate reinforcement ratio:

From Equation 4-131,

$$\begin{aligned}&p=A_{s}/bd\\&p_{N}=2.2/(18\times27.125)=0.0045\\&p_{p}=2.2/(18\times27.615)=0.0044\\ \end{aligned}$$

C.Maximum reinforcement:

Maximum reinforcing ratio $p_{m a x}=0.75~x~p_{b}$ 

From Equation 4-132;

$$p_{b}=\left[\frac{0.85K_{1}f_{dc}^{\prime}}{f_{dy}}\right]\cdot\left[\frac{87.000}{87.000+f_{dy}}\right]$$

where:

$$\begin{aligned}K_{1}&=0.85-\frac{0.05(f_{dc}^{\prime}-4.000)}{1.000}=0.812\\p_{b}&=\frac{0.85\times0.812\times4760}{77.220}\left(\frac{87.000}{87.000+77.220}\right)=0.0225\\p_{max}&=0.75\times0.0225=0.0169>p_{N}=0.0045\mathrm{~and}\\p_{p}&=0.0044\mathrm{~O.K.}\end{aligned}$$

d.Check minimum reinforcing ratio against the larger value from equations 4-138 and 4-138a:

$$p_{min}=\frac{200}{f_y}=\frac{200}{66,000}=0.0030$$

$$p_{min}=\frac{3\sqrt{f_c^{\prime}}}{f_v}=\frac{3\sqrt{4,000}}{66,000}=0.0029$$

<!-- batch 25 | page 34 -->

$$p_{min}=0.0030<p_N=0.0045$$

Step 5. Moment capacity of the beam using Equations 4-129 and 4-130 is:

$$M_{u}=A_{s}f_{d v}(d-a/2)$$

where:

$$\begin{aligned}a=\frac{A_{s}f_{dy}}{0.85bf_{dc}^{\prime}}\\a=\frac{2.20\times77,220}{0.85\times18\times4.760}=2.333in\end{aligned}$$

at support:

$$\begin{aligned}M_{N}&=2.20\times77,220\times(27.125-2.333/2)\\&=4.409.934in-lbs\end{aligned}$$

at mid-span:

$$\mathrm{M_{p}}=2.20\times77,220\times(27.625-2.333/2)$$

Step 6.From Table 3-1 (+), ultimate resistance of a uniformly loaded beam with fixed ends is:

$$\begin{aligned}&r_{u}=\frac{8\left(M_{N}+M_{p}\right)}{L^{2}}\\ &r_{u}=\frac{8\left(4.409,934+4,494,934\right)}{240^{2}}=1,236.79\mathsf{Ibs/in}\\ \end{aligned}$$

Step 7.From Section 4-15, calculate average moment of inertia of the beam section.



a.Concrete modulus of elasticity:

$$\begin{aligned}&E_{c}=w^{1.5}\times33\times(f_{c}^{\prime})^{1/2}\\&E_{c}=150^{1.5}\times33\times(4,000)^{1/2}=3.8\times10^{6}\mathrm{psi}\\ \end{aligned}$$

b.Steel modulus of elasticity:

$$E_{s}=29\times10^{6}\mathrm{p s i}$$

C.Modular ratio 

<!-- batch 25 | page 35 -->

$$\begin{aligned}&n=\frac{E_{s}}{E_{c}}\\&n=\frac{29\times10^{6}}{3.8\times10^{6}}=7.6\\ \end{aligned}$$

d.From Figure 4-11 (+) and having n,$p_{N}$ and $p_{p},$ the coefficients for moment of inertia of cracked sections are:

$$F_{N}=0.0235at support $$

$$F_{p}=0.0230\text{at mid-span}$$

Cracked moment of inertia from Equation 4-8b is:

$$\begin{aligned}&I_{c}=Fbd^{3}\\&I_{cN}=0.0235\times18\times27.125^{3}=8,442\;in^{4}\\&I_{cp}=0.0230\times18\times27.625^{3}=8,728\;in^{4}\\&Average:\\&I_{c}=(I_{cN}+I_{cp})/2\\&I_{c}=(8,442+8,728)/2=8,585\;in^{4}\\ \end{aligned}$$

e.Gross moment of inertia 

(Equation 4-8)

$$\begin{aligned}&I_{g}=\frac{bh^{3}}{12}\\&I_{g}=\frac{18\times30^{3}}{12}=40,500in^{4}\\ \end{aligned}$$

f.Average moment of inertia of the beams from Equation 4-7:

$$\begin{aligned}I_{a}=\frac{I_{g}+I_{c}}{2}\\ =\frac{40.500+8.585}{2}=24.542.5\ln^{4}\end{aligned}$$

Step 8.From Table 3-8 (+))$K_{E}$ of a uniformly loaded beam with fixed ends is:

$$\begin{aligned}K_{E}=&\frac{307E_{c}I_{a}}{L^{4}}\\=&\frac{307\times3.8\times10^{6}\times24.542.5}{240^{4}}\\=&8.629.70\mathrm{~lbs}/\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{in}\end{aligned}$$

<!-- batch 25 | page 36 -->

Step 9.

Equivalent elastic deflection from Equation 3-36 is:

$$X_{E}=\frac{r_{u}}{K_{E}}=\frac{1.236.79}{8.629.70}=0.1433\ln $$

Step 10.

Load-mass factor from Table 3-12 (+) for a plastic range of a uniformly loaded beam with fixed ends is:

$$\begin{aligned}&K_{LM}&\text{-elastic}&=0.77\\&&\text{-elastic-phase}&=0.78\\&&\text{-plastic}&=0.66\\ \end{aligned}$$

$K_{L M}$ for plastic mode deflections; from Section 3-17.4 from Chapter 3:

$$K_{LM}=\left[\left[\frac{0.77+0.78}{2}\right]+0.66\right]/2=0.72$$

Step 11.Natural period of the beam from Equation 3-60 is:

$$T_{N}=2\pi(K_{L M}m/K_{E})^{1/2}$$

Where m is the mass of the beam plus 20% of the slabs span perpendicular to the beam:

$$\begin{aligned}m&=\frac{W}{g}\\&=\left(30\times18+2\times8\times102\times0.20\right)\times\frac{150}{12^3}\times\frac{1000^2}{32.2\times12^3}\\&=194,638.50\;lbs-m^2/i n^2\\T_n&=2\pi\left[\frac{0.72\times194,638.50}{8,629.70}\right]^{1/2}=25.3\;ms\end{aligned}$$

Step 12.Find $\mu,$ ductility ratio from Figure 3-54 (+)

From Step 1:

$$\begin{aligned}\mathsf{T}/\mathsf{T}_{\mathsf{N}}&=60.7/25.3=2.40\\\mathsf{P}&=(18+84+120)\times7.2\\&=1,598.40\mathsf{Ibs}/\mathsf{in}\\r_{u}/\mathsf{P}&=\frac{1,236.79}{1,598.40}=0.77\\\mu&=9.0\end{aligned}$$

<!-- batch 25 | page 37 -->

Step 13. From Table 3-5 (+) support rotation is:

$$\begin{aligned}&X_{m}=\frac{L\tan\Theta}{2}\\&=\mu\times X_{E}\\&=9.0\times0.1433=1.29in\\&\tan\Theta=\frac{2\times1.29}{240}=0.01075\\&\Theta=0.620^{\circ}<2^{\circ}\quad Q.K.\\ \end{aligned}$$

Step 14.Direct Shear from Table 3-9 (+) is:

$$\begin{aligned}V_{s}=&\frac{r_{u}L}{2}\\ =&\frac{1236.79\times240}{2}=148,415lb\end{aligned}$$

Section capacity in direct shear from Equation 4-142:

$$\begin{aligned}&V_{d}=0.18\ f_{dc}\ bd\\&V_{d}=0.18\times4.400\times18\times27.125\\&\quad\quad\quad\quad=386.694\ lb>V_{s}=148.415\ O.K.\end{aligned}$$

Step 15.Diagonal tension stress from Equation 4-139:

$$v_{u}=\frac{V_{u}}{b d}\leq10(f_{c}^{\prime})^{1/2}$$

Total shear d distance from the face of support:

$$\begin{aligned}V_{u}&=(L/2-d)r_{u}\\&=\left(\frac{240}{2}-27.125\right)1236.79=114.867lbs;\\V_{u}&=\frac{114.867}{18\times27.125}=235.2psi\\10(f_{dc}^{\prime})^{1/2}&=10\times(4.000)^{1/2}\\&=632.5psi>235.2psi O.K.\end{aligned}$$

<!-- batch 25 | page 38 -->

Step 16.Unreinforced web shear capacity using Equation 4-140 is:

$$\begin{aligned}\boldsymbol{v}_{c}&=\left[1.9\left(\boldsymbol{f}_{dc}^{\prime}\right)^{1/2}+2.500\boldsymbol{p}\right]\\<&3.5\left(\boldsymbol{f}_{dc}^{\prime}\right)^{1/2}\\\boldsymbol{v}_{c}&=\left[1.9\left(4.000\right)^{1/2}+2.500\times0.0045\right]\\=&131.4\mathrm{~psi}\\(3.5\left(\boldsymbol{f}_{dc}^{\prime}\right)^{1/2}&=3.5\times\left(4.000\right)^{1/2}\\=&221.4\mathrm{~psi}>131.4\mathrm{~psi}\quad\mathrm{O.K.}\end{aligned}$$

Step 17.Area of web reinforcing from Equation 4-141:

$$\begin{aligned}&A_{v}=\left[\left(v_{u}-v_{c}\right)\times b\times s_{s}\right]/\phi\times f_{dy};\quad v_{u}-v_{c}\geq v_{c}\\&v_{u}-v_{c}=235.2-131.4=104<v_{c}\quad\mathrm{use}\quad v_{c}\\ \end{aligned}$$

Assume:

$$\begin{aligned}&s_{s}=9in\\&A_{v}=131.4\times18\times9/(0.85\times66,000)\\&\quad=0.38in/9in^2\\ \end{aligned}$$

Use No. 4 tie:

$$A_{v}=0.40\mathrm{i n}^{2}$$

Step 18.Minimum tie reinforcing area:

$$\begin{aligned}A_{v}(min)&=0.0015bs_{s}\\&=0.0015\times18\times9\\&=0.24in^{2}\leq0.40\ln^{2}O.K.\end{aligned}$$

Maximum tie spacing:

$$\begin{aligned}\left(\boldsymbol{f}_{dc}^{\prime}\right)^{1/2}&=4\times(4,000)^{1/2}\\&=253\mathrm{ps}i>v_{c}=131\mathrm{ps}i\\&>v_{u}-v_{c}=104\mathrm{ps}i\\s_{max}&=d/2\\&=27.125/2=13.56\mathrm{ln}>9\mathrm{in}\Omega\Omega\Omega\Omega\Omega\Omega\end{aligned}$$

$$r^{-}/r_{u}=0.50\ for\ T/T_{N}=2.40\ and\ X_{m}/X_{E}=9.0$$

$$r^{-}=0.50\times1236.79=618.4lbs/in $$

### PROBLEM 4A-7. DESIGN OF A BEAM SUBJECT TO TORSION 

### Problem:Design a beam for a uniformly distributed torsional load.Procedure:

Step 1.Design the beam and adjacent slabs in flexure for the applied blast load.Step 2.Calculate the unbalanced slab support shears,$V_{T}$ using the ultimate resistance of the slabs from Step 1.



$$V_{T}=\frac{r_{u1}L_{1}}{2}+\frac{r_{u2}L_{2}}{2}$$

Using the unbalanced slab support shears, compute the torsional load at d distance from the face of the support from:

$$T_{u}=\left(\frac{L}{2}-d\right)\frac{b}{2}V_{T}$$

Step 3.Using the torsional load from Step 2, compute the nominal torsional stress in the vertical direction from Equation 4-143 and in the horizontal direction from Equation 4-144. Verify that the torsional stresses do not exceed the maximum stress permitted in Section 4-41.5.



Note: If the height of the beam is greater than width, the horizontal torsional stresses will not be critical and may be ignored.

Step 4.Determine the shear and torsional capacity of an unreinforced web,$v_{c}$ and $v_{t c}$ ,from Equations 4-145 and 4-146 or 4-147, the torsional stress from Step 3 and the shear stress from Step 1.



Step 5.Find the excess shear stress $(V_{u}-V_{c})$ where the nominal shear stress $v_{u}$ is from Step 1, and the shear capacity of the unreinforced web $v_{c}$ is from Step 4. Using the excess shear stress and Equation 4-141, determine the area of web reinforcing for shear.



Step 6.With torsional capacity of the concrete from Step 4, the torsional stresses from Step 3, and Equations 4-148 and 4-149, calculate the area of web reinforcement for torsion in the vertical and, if required, in the horizontal directions.



Step 7.Add the area of shear reinforcement from Step 5 and the area of torsion reinforcement in the vertical direction, and compare with the area of torsion reinforcement required in the horizontal direction. The larger of the two values will control for the design of the closed ties. (If height of beam is greater than width, see note at Step 3.)



<!-- batch 25 | page 41 -->

Step 8.Check minimum area and maximum spacing requirements of ties according to Section 4-41.5.



Step 9.Calculate the required area of longitudinal torsion reinforcement from Equations 4-151a and 4-151b, the torsional stress from Step 3 and the torsion capacity of concrete from Step 4.



Step 10.Determine the distribution of flexural and longitudinal steel at the supports and at the midsection.



<!-- batch 25 | page 42 -->

### EXAMPLE 4A-7. DESIGN OF BEAM IN TORSION 

Required:Design of beam in example 4A-6, for torsional load due to unequal spans of adjacent slab.



Solution:

Step 1.Given:

a.Beam designed for flexure in example 4A-6 where:

$$\begin{aligned}&L=240in\\&d=27.125in;\quad b=18in;\\&v_{u}=235.2psi\\ \end{aligned}$$

b.Slabs designed for flexure where:

$$r_{u1}=15.0\mathrm{psi}L_1=14\mathrm{ft}=168\mathrm{in}$$

Step 2.Calculate torsional load.

a.Unbalanced slab support shears:

$$\begin{aligned}V_{T}&=\frac{r_{u1}L_{1}}{2}-\frac{r_{u2}L_{2}}{2}\\&=\frac{15\times168}{2}-\frac{7.85\times240}{2}=320\mathrm{~lb/in}\end{aligned}$$

b.Torsional load at d from the support:

$$\begin{aligned}T_{u}&=\left(\frac{L}{2}-d\right)\frac{b}{2}V_{T}\\&=\left(\frac{240}{2}-27.125\right)\times\frac{18}{2}\times320=267,480in-1b\end{aligned}$$

Step 3.Maximum torsional stress:

Since $h>b$ ,the torsional stress in the horizontal direction is not critical and will be ignored.



a.Torsional stress:

$$\begin{aligned}v_{(tu)V}=&\frac{3T_u}{b^2h}\\=&\frac{3\times267,480}{18^2\times30}=82.5\mathrm{psh}\end{aligned}$$

<!-- batch 25 | page 43 -->

b.Shear stress:$v_{u}=235.2\;\mathrm{p s}$ 

C.Check maximum allowable torsional stress.

$$\begin{aligned}maxv_{tu}=\frac{12\left(f_{dc}^{\prime}\right)^{1/2}}{\left[1+\left[\frac{1.2v_{u}}{v_{tu}}\right]^{2}\right]^{1/2}}\\=\frac{12\times4.000^{1/2}}{\left[1+\left[\frac{1.2\times235.2}{82.5}\right]^{2}\right]^{1/2}}=212.9>82.5\ O.K.\end{aligned}$$

Step 4.Find shear and torsional capacity of unreinforced web.

a.Shear capacity:

$$v_{c}=\frac{2(f_{_{d c}}^{\prime})^{1/2}}{\left[1+\left[\displaystyle\frac{v_{_{t u}}}{1.2v_{_{u}}}\right]^{2}\right]^{1/2}}$$

$$v_{c}=\frac{2\times4.000^{1/2}}{\left[1+\left[\frac{82.5}{1.2\times235.2}\right]^{2}\right]^{1/2}}=121.4\mathrm{~ps}i $$

b.Torsional capacity:

$$v_{t c}=\frac{2.4(f_{\it_{d c}}^{\prime})^{1/2}}{\left[1+\left[\displaystyle\frac{1.2v_{\it_u}}{v_{\it_{t u}}}\right]^{2}\right]^{1/2}}$$

$$v_{tc}=\frac{2.4\times4.000^{1/2}}{\left[1+\left[\frac{1.2\times235.2}{82.5}\right]^2\right]^{1/2}}$$

Step 5.Area of web reinforcing for shear using Equation 4-141:

$$\boldsymbol{A}_{v}=\left(\boldsymbol{v}_{u}-\boldsymbol{v}_{c}\right)\times\boldsymbol{b}\times\boldsymbol{s}/\left(\phi\times\boldsymbol{f}_{d v}\right)$$

Assume $s=12$ in 

<!-- batch 25 | page 44 -->

$$\begin{aligned}A_{v}&=(235.2-121.4)\times18\times12/(0.85\times66,000)\\&=0.438\ln^{2}/\mathrm{ft}\end{aligned}$$

Step 6.Web reinforcing for torsional stress using Equation 4-148:

$$A_{t}=\frac{\left(v_{tu}-v_{tc}\right)b^{2}h s}{3\phi\alpha_{t}b_{t}h_{t}f_{dv}}\quad Vertical $$

where:

$$\begin{aligned}\alpha_{t}=&0.66+0.33\frac{h_{t}}{b_{t}}\leq1.50\\h_{t}=&30.0-2.0-1.5-(2\times0.5/2)=26in\\&See figure4A-15.\\b_{t}=&18.0-1.5-1.5-(2\times0.5/2)=14.5in\\&See figure4A-15.\\\alpha_{t}=&0.66+0.33\frac{26}{14.5}=1.25<1.50\quad O.K.\\A_{t}=&\frac{(82.5-42.6)\times18^2\times30\times12}{3\times0.85\times1.25\times14.5\times26\times66,000}\\=&0.059in^2/H\end{aligned} 1$$

Step 7.Total web reinforcement:

$$A_{t}+A_{v}/2=0.059+0.438/2=0.278\ln^{2}/\mathrm{ft/Leg}$$

Use No.4 ties @ $3\;\mathrm{i n}=0.300\;\mathrm{i n}^{2}/\mathrm{f t}/\mathrm{L e g}$ J.

Step 8.Minimum torsion reinforcement (Section 4-41.5):

a.Minimum tie reinforcing area:

$A_{v}(\mathrm{m i n})=A_{v}$ . shear alone from example 4A-6

Use No.4 ties @ 9 in 

$$A_{v}(min)=\frac{0.38\times\frac{12}{9}}{0.253\times10^{2}/\mathrm{ft/Leg}}<0.300\mathrm{in^{2}/ft/Leg}$$

<!-- batch 25 | page 45 -->

b.Maximum spacing:

$$\begin{aligned}s_{max}&=\frac{h_t+b_t}{4}\\&=\frac{26+14.5}{4}\\&=10.125in>7in\quad Q.K.\end{aligned}$$

Step 9.Required area of longitudinal steel is the greater of the two values from Equations 4-151a or 4-151b.



$$\begin{aligned}A_{1}&=2A_{t}\times\frac{b_{t}+h_{t}}{s}\\&=2\times0.06\times\frac{14.5+26.0}{12}=0.40in^{2}\end{aligned}$$

$$\boldsymbol{A}_{1}=\left[\frac{400\times\boldsymbol{b}\times\boldsymbol{s}}{f_{d y}}\frac{\boldsymbol{v}_{t u}}{\boldsymbol{v}_{t u}+\boldsymbol{v}_{u}}-2\boldsymbol{A}_{t}\right]\times\frac{\boldsymbol{b}_{t}+\boldsymbol{h}_{t}}{\boldsymbol{s}}$$

where:

$$\frac{2A_{t}}{f_{d_{y}}}=\frac{50bs}{f_{d_{y}}}=\frac{50\times18\times12}{66.000}=0.16\ln^{2}/\mathrm{ft}=2A_{t}\\A_{1}=\left[\frac{400\times18\times12}{66.000}\frac{82.5}{82.5+235.2}-0.12\right]\times\frac{14.5+26.00}{12}\\=0.74\ln^{2}$$

Step 10.Distribute $A_{I},A_{s},$ ,and $A_{s}\bar{}$ as follows (see Figure 4A-16 (+)):

Distribute $A_{l}$ equally between four corners of the beam and one on each face of depth, a total of six locations to satisfy maximum spacing of 12inches.



$$A/6=0.74/6=0.12\mathrm{i n}^{2}$$

Vertical Face:

$$\begin{aligned}&\mathrm{One}\ (1)\ \mathrm{No.4\ bar}\\ &=0.20\ \mathrm{in}^2>0.12\ \mathrm{O.K.}\\ \end{aligned}$$

<!-- batch 25 | page 46 -->

Horizontal Face at Top:

$$\begin{aligned}&\mathrm{Support}=2.20(\mathrm{bending})+2\times0.12(\mathrm{torsion})\\ &\quad=2.44\mathrm{in}^2\\ \end{aligned}$$

$$\begin{aligned}&\mathsf{Two}(2)\;\mathsf{No.7\;at\;corners+three\;(3)\;No.6}\\ &\quad\;=2.52\;\mathsf{in}^2\;\mathsf{O.K.}\\ \end{aligned}$$

$${\mathrm{M i d s p a n}}=1.64{\mathrm{~(r e b o u n d)}}$$

$$\begin{aligned}&\mathrm{Two}(2)\mathrm{No.7at corners+one}(1)\mathrm{No.6}\\ &\quad=1.64\mathrm{in^2}\mathrm{O.K.}\\ \end{aligned}$$

Horizontal Face at Bottom:

$$\begin{array}{c}{{\mathrm{S u p p o r t}=\mathrm{G r e a t e r~o f~r e b o u n d~}(1.64\;\mathrm{i n}^{2})}}\\ {{\mathrm{o r~t o r s i o n~}(2\;x\;0.12)}}\end{array}$$

$$\begin{aligned}&\mathrm{Two}(2)\mathrm{No}.7\mathrm{at}\mathrm{corresponds}+\mathrm{one}(1)\mathrm{No}.6\\ &\quad=1.64\mathrm{in}^2\mathrm{O}.K.\\ \end{aligned}$$

Midspan = 2.20 (bending)

Two (2) No. 7 at corners + one (1) No. 6 + two (2) No. 5

$$2.26\ in^2>2.20\ O.K.$$

### PROBLEM 4A-8. COLUMN DESIGN 

### Problem:Design an interior column of a one-story structure with shear walls.Procedure:

Step 1.Establish design parameters:

a.End conditions of column.

b.Clear height of column.

C.Dynamic loads from roof.

d.Static material properties.

Step 2.Find equivalent static loads on the column by increasing the dynamic loads 20 percent (Section 4-47).



Step 3.From Table 4-1 (+), determine the dynamic increase factors, DIF. Using the DIF, the material properties from Step 1d and Equation 4-3, calculate the dynamic design strength of the concrete and the reinforcement.

Step 4.Assume a column section and reinforcing steel.

Step 5.Calculate the slenderness ratio of the column section assumed in Step 4,using either Equation 4-167 or 4-168. If the slenderness ratio is less than 22, slenderness effects may be neglected. If it is greater than 22 and less than 50, the moment magnifier must be calculated from Equation 4-170and the moments increased according to Equation 4-169. The column section must be increased if the slenderness ratio is greater than 50.

Step 6.Divide the moment by the axial load to obtain the design eccentricity in both directions. Verify that the design eccentricities are greater than the minimum eccentricity of 0.1h for a tied column and 0.0707D for a spiral column.



Step 7.Compute the balanced eccentricity $\mathbf{e}_{b}$ of the column using Equation 4-156or 4-158 (for a rectangular and circular column, respectively), the dynamic material properties from Step 3, and the section properties from Step 4.Compare the balanced eccentricity with the design eccentricity from Step 6.Determine if the column failure is controlled by compressive strength of the concrete.$(\mathbf{\mathit{e}}_{b}>\mathbf{\mathit{e}})$ or tensile strength of reinforcement $(\mathbf{\mathit{e}}_{b}<\mathbf{\mathit{e}})$ J.

Step 8.Calculate the ultimate axial load capacity, at the actual eccentricity, in both directions. If compression controls use Equation 4-160 or 4-161. If tension controls use Equation 4-162 or 4-166.



Step 9.Using Equation 4-154, compute the pure axial load capacity of the section.

<!-- batch 25 | page 49 -->

Step 10.Compute the ultimate capacity of the column section, using the load capacities at the actual eccentricities from Step 8, the pure axial load capacity from Step 9, and Equation 4-176. Verify that the ultimate load capacity is greater than the equivalent static load from Step 2.

Step 11.Provide ties according to Section 4-48.4 for a tied column, or Section 449.4 for a spiral reinforced column.



<!-- batch 25 | page 50 -->

### EXAMPLE 4A-8. COLUMN DESIGN 

## Required:Design of a rectangular, tied interior column

Solution:

Step 1.Given:

a.Both ends of column fixed 

b.Clear height of column,$I=120in$ 

C.Axial load, 491,000 Ibs 

Moment about x-axis, 2,946,000 in-Ibs 

No calculated moment about y-axis 

d.Reinforcing stel,$f_{v}=66,000\;\mathrm{p s i}$ Concrete,$f_{c}^{\prime}=4,000$ psi 

Step 2.Equivalent static loads.

a.Axial load 

$$P=491,000\times1.2=589,200\ lb $$

b.Moment about x-axis 

$$M_{x}=2.946,000\times1.2=3.535,200in-lbs $$

C.Moment about y-axis 

$$M_{v}=0\ in-1bs $$

Step 3.Dynamic material strengths:

a.Reinforcing Steel.

$$\begin{aligned}&f_{dy}=f_{y}\times DIF\\&f_{dy}=66,000\times1.10=72,600\mathrm{psi}\\ \end{aligned}$$

b.Concrete.

$$\begin{aligned}&f_{dc}^{\prime}=f_{c}^{\prime}\times DIF\\&f_{dc}^{\prime}=4,000\times1.12=4,480\mathrm{ps}\\ \end{aligned}$$

Step 4.Use an $18\times18$ column section with 12 No.7 reinforcing bars (see Figure 4A-17 (+)).



<!-- batch 25 | page 51 -->

<div style="text-align: center;">Figure 4A-17</div>


<div style="text-align: center;"><img src="../pages/batch 25/imgs/img_in_image_box_315_313_884_830.jpg" alt="Image" width="47%" /></div>


Radius of gyration for rectangular section is equal to 0.3 of depth.

$$r_{x}=r_{v}=0.3\times18=5.4\ in $$

From Section 4-46:

$$\frac{kI}{r}=\frac{0.9\times120}{5.4}=20<22$$

Therefore slenderness effects may be neglected.

Minimum eccentricity in both directions,

$$\begin{aligned}&e_{min}=0.1\times18=1.8in\\&e_x=M_x/P=3,535,200/589,200=6in>1.8in O.K.\\&e_y=M_y/P=0/589,200=0<1.8,use1.8in\\ \end{aligned}$$

Step 7.Balanced eccentricity:

$$\begin{aligned}&a.\quad&&\mathsf{From}\;\mathsf{Figure}\;4\mathsf{A}-17\;\underline{(+)},\\&&&&d_x=d_y=18-1.5-0.5-0.875/2\;=\;15.56\;\mathsf{in}\\&&&&A_{sx}=5\;x\;0.6=3.00\;\mathsf{in}^2\end{aligned}$$

<!-- batch 25 | page 52 -->

$$\begin{aligned}&A_{sy}=3\times0.6=1.80\;in^2\\\mathsf{b.}\quad&\mathsf{Find\;value\;of\;}m,\\&m=f_{dy}/(0.85\;\bar{f}_{dc})=72,600/(0.85\times4.480)\;=\;19.06\\\mathsf{c.}\quad&\mathsf{Usin g\;Equation\;4-156,}\\&e_{b}=\;0.20\;h+1.54\;A_{s}\;m/b\\&e_{bx}=0.20\;x\;18+(1.54\;x\;3.00\;x\;19.06)/18\\&\qquad=8.49\;in>6\;in,compressing controls\\&e_{by}=0.20\;x\;18+(1.54\;x\;1.80\;x\;19.06)/18\\&\qquad=6.54\;in>1.8\;in,compression controls\end{aligned} A_{sv}=3\times0.6=1.80in^2 \begin{aligned}&A_{sy}=3\times0.6=1.80\;in^2\\\mathsf{b.}\quad&\mathsf{Find\;value\;of\;}m,\\&m=f_{dy}/(0.85\;\bar{f}_{dc})=72,600/(0.85\times4.480)\;=\;19.06\\\mathsf{c.}\quad&\mathsf{Usin g\;Equation\;4-156,}\\&e_{b}=\;0.20\;h+1.54\;A_{s}\;m/b\\&e_{bx}=0.20\;x\;18+(1.54\;x\;3.00\;x\;19.06)/18\\&\qquad=8.49\;in>6\;in,compressing controls\\&e_{by}=0.20\;x\;18+(1.54\;x\;1.80\;x\;19.06)/18\\&\qquad=6.54\;in>1.8\;in,compression controls\end{aligned}$$

Step 8.Axial load from Equation 4-160:

$$P_{u}=\frac{A_{s}f_{dy}}{e/(2d-h)+0.5}+\frac{bhf_{dc}}{3he/d^{2}+1.18}$$

a.When only eccentricity $\pmb{\epsilon}_{x}$ is present:

$$\begin{aligned}&P_{x}=\frac{3.0\times72,600}{6/(2\times15.56-18)+0.5}\\ &+\frac{18\times18\times4,480}{3\times18\times6/15.56^{2}+1.18}=803,9191\mathrm{bs}\\ \end{aligned}$$

b.When only eccentricity $\pmb{\mathscr{e}}_{y}$ is present:

$$\frac{1.8\times72,600}{1.8/(2\times15.56-18)+0.5}\times\frac{1.8\times18\times4.480}{3\times18\times1.8/15.56^2+1.18}=957,137\mathrm{~Ibs}$$

Step 9.Compute pure axial load capacity from Equation 4-154.

$$\begin{aligned}P_{o}&=0.85\;f_{dc}^{\prime}\left(A_{g}-A_{st}\right)+A_{st}f_{dy}\\A_{g}&=18\;x\;18=324\;in^{2}\\A_{st}&=12\;x\;0.6=7.2\;in^{2}\\P_{o}&=0.85\;x\;4480\;(324-7.2)+7.2\;x\;72,6000\\&=1,729,094\;lbs\end{aligned}$$

<!-- batch 25 | page 53 -->

Step 10.Ultimate capacity of the column from Equation 4-176:

$$\frac{1}{P_{u}}=\frac{1}{P_{x}}+\frac{1}{P_{y}}-\frac{1}{P_{o}}=\frac{1}{\frac{758,417}{\frac{1}{\frac{1}{\frac{1}{\frac{1}{\frac{1}{\frac{1}{\frac{1}{\frac{1}{\frac{1}{\frac{1}{\left(1+1)}}}}}}}}}}}}=\frac{1}{\frac{1}{\frac{1}{\frac{1}{\frac{1}{\frac{1}{\frac{1}{\left(1+1\right)}}}}}}}=\frac{1}{\frac{1}{\frac{1}{\frac{1}{\frac{1}{\left(1+1\right)}}}}}=\frac{1}{\frac{1}{\frac{1}{\frac{1}{\left(1+1\right)}}}}=\frac{1}{\frac{1}{\frac{1}{\left(1+1\right)}}}=\frac{1}{\frac{1}{\frac{1}{\left(1+1\right)}}}=\frac{1}{\frac{1}{\frac{1}{\left(1+1\right)}}}=\frac{1}{\frac{1}{\frac{1}{\left(1+1\right)}}}=\frac{1}{\frac{1}{\left(1+1\right)}}=\frac{1}{\frac{1}{\left(1+1\right)}}=\frac{1}{\frac{1}{\left(1+1\right)}}=\frac{1}{\frac{1}{\left(1+1\right)}}=\frac{1}{\frac{1}{\left(1+1\right)}}=\frac{1}{\frac{1}{\left(1+1\right)}}=\frac{1}{\frac{1}{\left(1+1\right)}}=\frac{1}{\frac{1}{\left(1+1\right)}}=\frac{1}{\frac{1}{\left(1+1\right)}}=\frac{1}{\frac{1}{\left(1+1\right)}}=\frac{1}{\frac{1}{\left(1+1\right)}}=\frac{1}{\frac{1}{\left(1+1\right)}}=\frac{1}{\frac{1}{\left(1+1\right)}}=\frac{1}{\frac{1}{\left(1+1\right)}}=\frac{1}{\frac{1}{\left(1+1\right)}}=\frac{1}{\left(1+1\right)}=\frac{1}{\left(1+1\right)}=\frac{1}{\left(1+1\right)}=\frac{1}{\left(1+1\right)}=\frac{1}{\left(1+1\right)}=\frac{1}{\left(1+1\right)}=\frac{1}{\left(1+1\right)}=\frac{1}{\left(1+1\right)}=\frac{1}{\left(1+1\right)}=\frac{1}{\left(1+1\right)}=\frac{1}{\left(1+1\right)}=\frac{1}{\left(1+1\right)}=\frac{1}{\left(1+1+1\right)}=\frac{1}{\left(1+1\right)}=\frac{1}{\left(1+1+1\right)}=\frac{1}{\left(1+1\right)}=\frac{1}{\left(1+1+1\right)}=\frac{1}{\left(1+1+1\right)}=\frac{1}{\left(1+1+1\right)}=\frac{1}{\left(1+1+1\right)}=\frac{1}{\left(1+1+1\right)}=\frac{1}{\left(1+1+1\right)}=\frac{1}{\left(1+1+1\right)}=\frac{1}{\left(1+1+1\right)}=\left(1+1+1\right)}=\frac{1}{\left(1+1+1\right)}=\frac{1}{\left(1+1+1+1\right)}=\frac{1}{\left(1+1+1\right)}=\frac{1}=\frac{1}{1}=\frac{1}{1}=\left(1+1+1+1\left(1+1\right)}=\f ### PROBLEM 4A-9. BRITTLE MODE, POST-FAILURE FRAGMENTS 

### Problem:Design an element, which responds to blast impulse, for controlled post-failure fragments. 

## Procedure:

Step 1.Establish design parameters:

a.Impulse load and duration (Chapter 2).

b.Maximum average velocity of post-failure fragments Vf as required by receiver sensitivity.



C.Geometry of element.

d.Support conditions.

e.Materials to be used and corresponding static design strengths.

f.Dynamic increase factors (Table 4-1 (+)).

Step 2.Determine dynamic yield strength and ultimate strength of reinforcement from Equation 4-3.



Step 3.Determine the dynamic design stress for the flexural reinforcement according to the deflection range (support rotation) from Table 4-2 (+)

Step 4.Substitute known quantities of $j_{b}$ (Step 1a),$f_{d s}$ (Step 3), H (Step 1c), and f (Step 1b) into Equation 4-194.



Step 5.Obtain optimum ratio of vertical to horizontal reinforcement $p_{V}/p_{H}$ ,for a given wall thickess $T_{c}$ .



a.Assume a value of $d_{c}$ and substitute it into the equation obtained in Step 4.



b.Read optimum:$p_{V}/p_{H}$ ratio from Figure 4-38 (+)

Note: For one-way elements, the ratio of the main to secondary reinforcement is always 4 to 1 unless minimum conditions govern (Table 4-3 (+)). Obtain $C_{u}$ from Table 4-11 (+) for the given support condition.$C_{f}$ is always equal to 22,500 (Section 4-58)



Step 6.For the optimum $p_{V}/p_{H}$ ratio, determine $C_{u}$ (from Figure 4-33 (+), 4-34 (+)or $4{\scriptstyle-35(\pm))}$ and $C_{f}$ (from Figure 4-73 (+), 4-74 (+) or 4-75 (+)).Calculate $p_{V}$ and $p_{H}$ . Select bar sizes and spacings necessary to furnish the required reinforcement ratios.



<!-- batch 26 | page 2 -->

Step 7.Determine the required lacing and diagonal bars. (Procedure is exactly the same as that for elements designed for the ductile mode for incipient failure or less. See Problem 4A-3.)



Step 8.Determine the required $T_{c}$ for the assumed $d_{c}$ selected flexural and lacing bar sizes and required concrete cover. Adjust $T_{c}$ to the nearest whole inch and calculate the actual $d_{c}$ 



Check flexural capacity of the element based on either blast impulse of post-failure fragment velocity. Generally, lacing bar sizes do not have to be checked since they are not usually affected by a small change in 1$d_{c}$ .

a.Compute the actual impulse capacity of the element using Equation 4-194 and compare with the anticipated blast load. Repeat design (from Step 5 on) if the capacity is less than that required.

b.Compute the actual post-failure fragment velocity using Equation 4194 and compare with the value permitted by the acceptor sensitivity. Repeat design (from Step 5 on) if the actual velocity is greater than that permitted.



Step 10.Determine whether the correct design procedure has been utilized by first computing the response time of the element $t_{u}$ (time to reach ultimate deflection) from Equation 4-192 or 4-193. Then compare the response time $t_{u}$ with the duration of the blast load $\mathfrak{t}_{\circ},$ .For elements that respond to impulse loading $t_{u}/t_{0}>3$ 



Note: To obtain the most economical design, repeat Steps 5 through 10for several wall thicknesses and compare their costs. Percentages of reinforcement can be used to reduce the amount of calculations. In determining the required quantities of reinforcement, the length of the lap splice should be considered.

### EXAMPLE 4A-9. BRITTLE MODE, POST-FAILURE FRAGMENTS 

## Required:Design the back wall of an interior cell (Figure 4A-18 (+)) of a multicubicle structure for controlled post-failure fragments

Solution:

Step 1.Given:

a.$i_{b}=4800\ \mathrm{psi-ms},\ \mathrm{and}\ t_{0}=1.0\ \mathrm{ms}$ 

b.$v_{f}=100\ \mathrm{fs}=1.2\ \mathrm{in/ms}$ 

C.$L=360in,\quad H=120in$ 

d.Fixed on three edges and one edge free 

e.Reinforcing bars;.$f_{V}=66,000$ psi and $f_{u}=90,000\ ps$ i 

Concrete,$f_{c}^{\prime}=4.000$ psi 

f.For reinforcement,$DIF=1.23$ for yield stress 

$DIF=1.05$ for ultimate stress 

<div style="text-align: center;">Figure 4A-18</div>

<div style="text-align: center;">PLAN </div>


<div style="text-align: center;">ELEVATION </div>


Step 2.Dynamic strength of materials.

$$\begin{aligned}&f_{dy}=DIFf_{y}=1.23\times66,000=81,180\mathrm{psi}\\ &f_{dy}=DIFf_{y}=1.05\times90,000=94,500\mathrm{psi}\\ \end{aligned}$$

Step 3.Dynamic design stress from Table 4-2 (+)

$$f_{ds}=\frac{f_{dy}+f_{du}}{2}=\frac{81.180+94.500}{2}=87.840\mathrm{psi}$$

<!-- batch 26 | page 4 -->

Step 4.Substitute known quantities into Equation 4-194.

$$\begin{aligned}{{I_{b}}^{2}}&{{}={C_{u}}\left[\frac{{{p_{H}}d_{c}^{3}{f_{d s}}}}{H}\right]+{C_{f}}d_{c}^{2}{v_{f}}^{2}}\\ {4800^{2}}&{{}=\frac{{{C_{u}}{p_{H}}d_{c}^{3}\times87,840}}{{120}}+{C_{f}}d_{c}^{2}\times1.2^{2}}\\ {23.04\times10^{6}}&{{}=732{C_{u}}{p_{H}}d_{c}^{3}+1.44{C_{f}}d_{c}^{2}}\end{aligned}$$

Step 5.Optimum reinforcement ratio.

a.Assume $d_{c}=21$ in and substitute into Equation 4-194.

$$23.04\times10^{6}=732C_{u}p_{H}(21)^{3}+1.44C_{f}(21)^{2}$$

Therefore:

$$p_{H}=\frac{23.04\times10^{6}-635\times C_{f}}{6.78\times10^{6}\times C_{u}}$$

b.Read optimum $p_{V}/p_{H}$ value from Figure 4-38 (+) for $L/H=3$ 

$$p_{V}/p_{H}=1.58$$

Step 6.For optimum $p_{V}/p_{H}=1.58$ 

From Figure 4-34 (+),$C_{u}=477$ 

From Figure 4-74 (+),$C_{f}=1.84\times10^{4}$ 

$$\begin{aligned}\boldsymbol{p}_{H}&=\frac{23.04\times10^{6}-635\times1.84\times10^{4}}{6.78\times10^{6}\times477}=0.00351\\\boldsymbol{p}_{V}&=1.58\boldsymbol{p}_{H}=1.58\times0.00351=0.00555\\\boldsymbol{A}_{stH}&=0.00555\times12\times21=1.398\ln^{2}/\mathrm{ft}\\&\quad\text{use}\#8\textcircled{o}6\text{in.}(\boldsymbol{A}_{s}=1.58\ln^{2})\\\boldsymbol{A}_{sv}&=0.00877\times12\times21=2.21\ln^{2}/\mathrm{ft}\\&\quad\text{use}\#10\textcircled{o}6\text{in.}(\boldsymbol{A}_{s}=2.54\ln^{2})\\\boldsymbol{A}&\text{ctuual}\frac{\boldsymbol{p}_{V}}{\boldsymbol{p}_{H}}=\frac{\boldsymbol{A}_{sV}}{\boldsymbol{A}_{stH}}=\frac{2.54}{1.58}=1.610\end{aligned}$$

Step 7.Using lacing method No. 3, No. 7 vertical lacing bars are required.(Calculations are not shown since they are similar to those presented in 

<!-- batch 26 | page 5 -->

example 4A-3 for incipient failure design. Also, the remainder of the design for shear will not be shown.)



Step 8.The actual $d_{c}$ depends upon the details of the base of the wall (region of vertical lacing).



$$\begin{array}{l}\text{Figure4A-19}\\\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text\text{}\text{}\text{}\text{}\text\text{}\text{}\text{}\text\text{}\text{}\text{}\text\text{}\text{}\text{}\text\text{}\text{}\text\text{{}\text}\text\text{{}\text}\text\text{{}\text}\text\text{{}\text}\text\text{{}\text\text}\text{{{}\text\text}\text\text{{}\text\text}\text\text{{{}\text\text}\text\text{ $$

$T_{c}=d_{c}+6.52=27.52$ in, use 28 in 

actual $d_{c}=28-6.52=21.48\ in$ 

Step 9.Actual capacity of element 

a.Actual impulse capacity 

$$\begin{aligned}&For p_{V}/p_{H}=1.610,\quad C_{u}=481\\&and\quad C_{f}=1.855\times10^{4}\\&d_{c}=21.48in\\&p_{H}=1.05/(12\times21.48)=0.00407\\ \end{aligned}$$

$$\begin{aligned}{i_{c}^{\quad2}}&{{}={\cal C}_{u}\left[\frac{p_{_{H}}d_{_{c}}^{\quad3}f_{_{d s}}}{H}\right]+{\cal C}_{r}d_{_{c}}^{\quad2}v_{_{r}}^{\quad2}}\\ {}&{{}=48\left[\frac{0.00407\times21.48^{3}\times87,840}{120}\right]+1.855\times10^{4}\times21.48^{2}\times1.2^{2}}\\ {}&{{}=26.752\times10^{6}}\\ {i_{c}}&{{}=5150\mathrm{p s i-m s}>i_{b}=4,800\mathrm{p s i-m s}\qquad\mathrm{O.K.}}\end{aligned}$$

<!-- batch 26 | page 6 -->

b.Actual post-failure fragment velocity 

$$\begin{aligned}i_{b}^{2}&=C_{u}\left[\frac{p_{H}d_{c}^{3}f_{ds}}{H}\right]+C_{r}d_{c}^{2}v_{f}^{2}\\4,&800^{2}=481\times\frac{0.00407\times21.48^{3}\times87,840}{120}+1.855\times10^{4}\times21.48^{2}\times v_{f}^{2}\\v_{f}&=1.10in/ms=92fps<100fps\quad O.K.\end{aligned}$$

Step 10.Response time of element $t_{u};$ ,

$$\begin{aligned}M_{VN2}&=M_{VP}and M_{HN1}=M_{HN1}=M_{HN3}=M_{HP}\\M_{HP}&=A_{sH}f_{ds}(d_c/b)=\frac{1.05\times87.840\times21.48}{12}\\&=165,095in-lbs/in\\M_{VP}&=A_{sv}f_{ds}d_c/b=\frac{1.69\times87.840\times21.48}{12}\\&=265,725in-lbs/in\end{aligned}$$

$$x_{2}/x_{1}=1.0(symmetric-field lines)$$

$$\begin{aligned}&\frac{L}{H}\left[\frac{M_{VP}}{M_{HN1}+M_{HP}}\right]^{1/2}=\frac{360}{120}\left[\frac{26.725}{2\times165.095}\right]^{1/2}=2.69\\ &\frac{M_{VP}}{M_{VN2}}=\frac{265.725}{265.725}=1.0\\ \end{aligned}$$

From Figure 3-11 (+),

$$\begin{aligned}&x_{1}/L=0.358\\&x_{1}=x_{2}=0.358(360)=128.88\\&r_{u}=\frac{5(M_{HN1}+M_{HP})}{{X_{1}}^{2}}for x_{1}<L/2\\&r_{u}=\frac{5(2×165,095)}{120}=99.4psi\\&m=225d_{c}=225(21.48)=4833psi-ms^2/min^2\\&r_{LM})_{u}=0.557\\&m_{u}=0.557(4833)=2692psi-ms^2/in\\&x_{1}=128.88>H=120\\ \end{aligned}$$

<!-- batch 26 | page 7 -->

Therefore, vertical supports fail first and the post-ultimate range resistance is:

$$\begin{aligned}&r_{up}=\frac{8(M_{HN1}+M_{HP})}{L^2}=\frac{8(2\times165,095)}{360^2}=20.4\mathrm{psi}\\ &m_{up}=0.66\times4833=3,190\mathrm{psi}-\mathrm{ms}^2/\mathrm{in}\\ &X_1=H\tan\theta_{\max}=12\times\tan12^\circ=25.51\mathrm{in}\\ \end{aligned}$$

Use Equation 4-192 for $t_{u}$ ,

$$\begin{aligned}t_{u}&=\frac{i_{b}}{m_{u}}+\left[\frac{m_{up}}{m_{u}r_{up}}-\frac{1}{r_{u}}\right]\left[i_{b}^{2}-2m_{u}r_{u}X_{1}\right]^{1/2}-\left[\frac{m_{u}}{r_{up}}\right]V_{t}\\t_{u}&=\frac{4.800}{2.692}+\left[\frac{3190}{2692\times20.4}-\frac{1}{99.4}\right]\left[4800^{2}-2\times2692\times99.4\times25.51\right]^{1/2}\\&-\left(\frac{2692}{20.4}\right)1.1=3.78ms\end{aligned}$$

$\frac{t_{u}}{t_{o}}=\frac{3.78}{1.0}=3.78>3$ , Therefore, correct procedure has been used

### PROBLEM 4A-10. MAXIMUM FRAGMENT PENETRATION 

## Problem:Determine the maximum penetration of a primary metal fragment into a concrete wall and determine if perforation occurs

## Procedure:

Step 1.Establish design parameters:

a.Type of fragment 

b.Weight Wf and diameter d of fragment 

C.Fragment striking velocity $v_{s}$ 

d.Thickness $T_{c}$ and the ultimate compressive strength $f_{c}$ of concrete wall 



Step 2.Determine the maximum penetration $X_{t}$ from Figure 4-78 (+) (or Equation 4-200 or 4-201) for the values of Wf and $v_{s}$ in Step 1. Xf is the maximum penetration of an armor-piercing steel fragment into 4,000 psi concrete.

Step 3.To determine the depth of penetration into concrete with ultimate srength other than 4,000 psi use $X_{t}$ from Step 2, the concrete strength from Step 1d and Equation 4-202:

$$X_{f}^{\prime}=X_{f}(4000/f_{c})^{1/2}$$

Step 4.To obtain the maximum penetration into concrete by metal fragments other than those of armor-piercing fragments use the penetration from Step 3, the penetrability coefficient k from Table 4-16 (+) and Equation 4203.



$$X_{f}^{\prime}=k X_{f}$$

Step 5.Calculate the limiting thickness of concrete at which perforation will occur from Equation 4-204.



$$T_{p f}=1.13\times d^{0.1}+1.311d $$

Where applicable, replace $X_{t}$ with $X_{t}$ from Step 3 or 4. If $T_{p f}$ is less than $T_{0}$ embedment will occur; if $T_{p f}$ is greater than $T_{c}$ the fragment will perforate the wall.



<!-- batch 26 | page 9 -->

### EXAMPLE 4A-10. MAXIMUM FRAGMENT PENETRATION 

Required:Maximum penetration of a primary fragment into a concrete wall;determine if perforation occurs.



Solution:

Step 1.Given:

a.Type of fragment: mild steel 

b.Primary fragment weight:$W_{f}=30ounces$ 

Primary fragment diameter:$d=2.15inche=$ 

C.Striking velocity:$v_{s}=3.500$ fps 

d.Thickness of wall:$T_{c}=18$ inches 

Ultimate concrete compressive strength:$f_{c}^{\prime}=4,000\mathrm{p s i}$ 

Step 2.Maximum penetration:

For:

$$\begin{aligned}&W_{f}=30\mathrm{~oz~and~}v_{s}=3,500\mathrm{~fps}\\&X_{f}=14.5\mathrm{~inches~from~Figure~4-78~(+)}\\ \end{aligned}$$

or from Equation 4-199

$$X_{f}=2.04\times10^{-6}d^{1.2}vs^{1.8}+d\geq2d $$

$$X_{f}>2d=2\times2.15=4.30\ 0.K.$$

Step 3.Concrete strength adjustment 

(Equation 4-202)

$$\begin{aligned}&X_{f}^{\prime}=X_{f}\left(4000/f_{c}^{\prime}\right)^{1/2}\\ &\quad=14.5\times1=14.5\text{in}\\ \end{aligned}$$

Step 4.Fragment material adjustment:

a.

$$k=0.70$$

b.

$$X_{f}^{\prime}=k X_{f}$$

$$0.7\times14.5=10.15in $$

<!-- batch 26 | page 10 -->

Step 5.Calculate minimum thickness to prevent perforation.

$$\begin{aligned}{T_{p f}}&{{}=1.13\;X_{f}^{\prime}d^{0.1}+1.311\;d}\\ {}&{{}=1.13\;(10.15)\;(2.15)^{0.1}+1.311\;(2.15)}\end{aligned}$$

$$T_{pf}=15.20in\leq18in $$

Since $T_{p f}$ is less than $T_{c}$ the fragment does not perforate the slab.

# PROBLEM 4A-11. DETERMINATION OF THE OCCURRENCE AND EFFECTS OF  PERFORATION 

### Problem:Determine the residual velocity of a primary fragment if it perforates a concrete wall. 

## Procedure:

Step 1.Determine the type, weight $W_{f},$ and striking velocity $v_{s}$ of the primary fragment. Also, the thickness of the concrete wall $T_{c}$ and the ultimate compressive stress $f_{c}$ of the concrete must be known.

Step 2.Proceed through Steps 2, 3, 4, and 5 of Problem 4A-10.If $T_{p f}$ is greater than $T_{c},$ perforation will occur.



Step 3.If perforation is not indicated by the calculations in Step 2 then discontinue the analysis. lf perforation does result, then compute the value of $T_{o}/T_{p f}$ 

Step 4.Utilizing the value of $T_{c}/T_{p f,}$ 。,obtain $v_{r}/v_{s}$ from Figure 4-79 (+) or 4-80 (+)

Step 5.With the values of $v_{s}$ and $v_{r}/v_{s}$ of Steps 1 and 4, calculate the residual velocity $v_{r}$ .



<!-- batch 26 | page 12 -->

### PROBLEM 4A-12. DETERMINATION OF THE OCCURRENCE OF SPALLING 

### Problem:To determine if spalling of a concrete wall occurs if there is no perforation by the primary fragment. 

## Procedure:

Step 1.Determine the type, weight $W_{f}$ and striking velocity $v_{s}$ of the primary fragment. Also, the thickness of the concrete wall and the ultimate compressive stress of the concrete $f_{c}$ must be known.

Step 2.Proceed through Steps 2, 3, 4, and 5 of Problem $4A{-}10$ 

Step 3.If embedment of the fragment occurs, compute the limiting concrete thickness at which spalling will occur according to:

$$T_{s o}=1.215X_{f}d^{0.1}+2.12d 一
$$

If $T_{s p}$ is greater than $T_{c},$  spalling will occur.

<!-- batch 26 | page 15 -->

### EXAMPLE 4A-12. DETERMINATION OF THE OCCURRENCE OF SPALLING 

### Required:Determine if spalling of a concrete wall occurs due to penetration by a primary fragment. 

Solution:

Step 1.Given:

a.Type of metal: armor-piercing steel 

b.Primary fragment weight:$W_{f}=40\text{ounces}$ 

C.Primary fragment diameter: 2.38 inches 

d.Striking velocity:$v_{s}=3{,}000\;\mathrm{f p s}$ 

e.Thickness of wall:$T_{c}=19$ inches 

f.Ultimate concrete compressive stress:$f_{c}^{\prime}=5,000\mathrm{p s}$ 

Step 2.For given conditions:

$$\begin{aligned}&X_{f}=12.8inche\\&X_{f}^{\prime}=12.8(4000/5000)^{2}\\&\quad=11.5in\\&k=1.00\\&X_{f}^{\prime}=X_{f}\\ \end{aligned}$$

Then:

$$\begin{aligned}&T_{pf}=1.13(11.5)(2.38)^{0.1}\\ &\quad+1.311(2.38)\\ &\quad=14.2+3.1=17.3inche\\ \end{aligned}$$

Since $T_{c}$ is greater than 17.3 inches, embedment occurs.

Step 3.Determine minimum concrete thickness to prevent spalling from Equation 4-207:

$$\begin{aligned}T_{sp}&=1.215(11.5)(2.38)^{0.1}+2.12(2.38)\\&=20.3inches\end{aligned}$$

Since $T_{s p}$ is greater than 19 inches, spalling will occur.

<!-- batch 26 | page 16 -->

# PROBLEM 4A-13. DETERMINATION OF THE EFFECTS OF A PRIMARY  FRAGMENT ON A COMPOSITE WALL 

Problem:Determine the maximum penetration by a primary fragment into a composite wall and the resulting effects on the donor panel, sand layer and acceptor panel.



## Procedure:

Step 1.Determine the type, weight $W_{f},$ and striking velocity $v_{s}$  of the primary fragment.$\mathsf{A l s o}$ ,the thicknesses of the concrete donor panel $T_{c}$ (donor),the sand layer $T_{s},$ and the concrete receiver panel $T_{c}$ (acceptor) and the ultimate compressive stress $f_{c}$ of the concrete must be known.

Step 2.Proceed through Steps 2, 3, 4, and 5 of Problem 4A-10.

If embedment of the fragment in the donor panel occurs, perform Step 3 of Problem 4A-12 to determine if spalling takes place.



If perforation of the donor panel occurs, compute:

$T_{c}$ (donor)$/T_{p f}$ 

and perform Steps 4 and 5 of Problem 4A-11 to find the residual velocity 

$v_{r}$ 



Step 3.Utilizing $v_{r}(\mathsf{d o n o r})$ as the $v_{s}$ of the sand layer and $W_{f},$ obtain $X_{s}$ from Figure 4-81 (+).



If $X_{s}$ is less than $T_{s}$ , the fragment is embedded in the sand layer and the analysis is discontinued.



If $X_{s}$ is greater than $T_{s},$ perforation of the sand occurs.

Step 4.Compute $T_{s}/X_{s}$ if perforation results. Use this value and Figure $4-80(\pm)$ to obtain $v_{r}/v_{s}$ of the sand layer.



Calculate v,(sand).

Step 5.Utilizing v,(sand) as the $v_{s}$ of the acceptor wall and $W_{f},$ obtain $X_{t}$ (acceptor) from Figure 4-78 (+).



Proceed through Steps 3 and 4 of Problem 4A-10, if necessary.

If embedment of the fragment in the acceptor panel occurs, perform Step 3 of Problem 4A-12 to determine if spalling takes place.

If perforation of the acceptor wall occurs, compute $T_{c}$ (acceptor) and perform Steps 4 and 5 of Problem 4A-11 to find $v_{r}$ 

<!-- batch 26 | page 17 -->

# EXAMPLE 4A-13. DETERMINATION OF THE EFFECTS OF A PRIMARY  FRAGMENT ON A COMPOSITE WALL 

Required:Maximum penetration by a primary fragment into a composite wall and the resulting effects on the donor panel, sand layer and the acceptor panel.



Solution:

Step 1.Given:

a.Type of metal: armor-piercing steel 

b.Primary fragment weight:$W_{f}=20ounces$ 0

C.Primary fragment diameter:$d=1.89inchees$

e.Thicknesses:

$$T_{c}(donor)=12inches $$

$$T_{s}=24inchees $$

$$T_{c}(acceptor)=12inthese $$

f.Ultimate concrete compressive stress:

$$f_{c}^{\prime}=5,000\mathrm{p s i}$$

Step 2.For given condittions:

$$\begin{aligned}&X_{f}=16.5inches\\&X_{f}^{\prime}=16.5(4000/5000)^{2}\\&\quad=14.7inches\\&k=1.00\\&X_{f}^{\prime}=X_{f}\\ \end{aligned}$$

Since $X_{t}^{r}$ is greater than 12 inches, perforation of the donor panel wil certainly occur.$T_{p f}$ must be calculated in order to determine the residual velocity of the fragment.



$$\begin{aligned}&T_{pf}=1.13(14.7)(1.89)^{0.1}+1.311(1.89)\\ &\quad=17.7+2.5=20.2inche\\&T_{c}/T_{pf}=12/20.2=0.594\\ \end{aligned}$$

<!-- batch 26 | page 18 -->

Since $\mathsf{X}_{\mathsf{p f}}$ is greater than 2d,$v_{r}/v_{s}=0.61$ from Figure 4-80 (+).

$$v_{r}(donor)=0.61(4,200)=2.562\mathrm{~fs}$$

Step 3.For:

$$\begin{aligned}&v_{s}(\mathrm{sand})=2,562\mathrm{fs},\\&X_{s}=64.0\mathrm{inches}\\ \end{aligned}$$

Since $T_{s}$ is less than 64.0 inches, perforation of the sand layer will occur.

Step 4.

$$T_{s}/X_{s}=24/64.0=0.375$$

from Figure 4-80 (+)

Step 5.

$$v_{r}/v_{s}=0.77\quad v_{r}(\mathrm{s}\mathrm{and})=0.77\quad v_{s}\quad v_{r}=0.77\quad(2,562)=1,970\quad\mathrm{fps}$$

Step 6.For:

$$\begin{aligned}&V_{s}\left(acceptor\right)=1,970\ fps\\&X_{f}\left(acceptor\right)=5.6\ inches\\&X_{f}^{\prime}=5.6\ (4000/5000)^{1/2}\\&\quad=5.0\ inches\\&k=1.00\\&X_{f}^{\prime}=X_{f}\\&T_{pf}=1.13\ (5.0)\ (1.89)^{0.1}\\&\quad+1.311\ (1.89)\\&\quad=6.0+2.5=8.5\ inches\\ \end{aligned} S $$

Since $T_{p f}$ is less than $T_{c}$ (acceptor), the fragment is embedded in the receiver panel. Calculate $T_{s p}$ to determine if spalling occurs:

$$\begin{aligned}&T_{sp}=1.215(5.0)(1.89)^{0.1}\\ &\quad+2.12(1.89)\\ &\quad=6.5+4.0=10.5inche\\ \end{aligned}$$

Since $T_{s p}$ is less than $T_{c}$ (acceptor), spalling will not occur.
